## November 23, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED EXPERIMENTAL TRAINING PRICES AND TEST METHOD (UPGRADE 3.1)

**Scope:** This is an approved experimental refinement of `training-1`, not a
replacement of operative wound combat, frozen `hp-d20-1`, working `hp-d20-2`,
equipment, Craft/SP/MP identities, live sheets or historical simulation evidence.

### Independent derived-statistic prices

Every derived statistic purchased with TP may have its own configurable escalating
price ladder, with explicit inclusive rank bands and a defined continuation formula
beyond those bands. Preserve the original training schedule as the default for
backward compatibility. Independent configurability does not approve a universal
numerical price or effect schedule for HP, damage, armor or other statistics.
Attribute-derived accuracy and evasion remain unchanged.

### Approved evasion experiment

Every purchased evasion rank grants **+1 evasion**. Use these experimental prices:

| Purchased ranks | TP per rank |
|---|---:|
| 1–4 | 2 |
| 5–6 | 8 |
| 7–8 | 14 |
| 9–10 | 22 |
| 11–12 | 32 |
| 13–14 | 44 |
| 15–16 | 58 |
| 17–18 | 74 |

Let `C(n)=2+b*(b+3)/2`, where `b=floor((n-1)/4)`, denote the
original four-rank price ladder. For each new purchased evasion rank `r>4`,
price is **`C(2*r-5)+C(2*r-4)`**. This combines two successive original
ranks after rank 4 and continues indefinitely: ranks 19–20 cost 92 TP each,
21–22 cost 112 TP each. There is no new game rank cap. Every purchase is useful;
do not implement fractional ranks or an evasion benefit only every second purchase.

This supersedes the older evasion prices **only when this approved experiment is
selected**. The original ladder remains the compatibility default; physical and
magical accuracy prices and all attribute-derived formulas remain unchanged
unless a separate explicit experiment selects other configurable prices.

### Standing assessment method

- Specialists spend approximately **80% of TP on their specialty and 20% on
  compensating for weaknesses**, including HP, evasion/AC and accuracy as relevant.
  Treat the split as approximate under discrete escalating prices.
- Offensive builds aim for accuracy comparable to the generalist reference,
  **exceeding the 20% support allocation when necessary**.
- Defensive specialists maintain meaningful offensive capabilities.
- Exclude deliberately dysfunctional pure specialists from routine balance
  assessments. Preserve explicit historical fixtures and isolated controls for
  reproduction; do not rewrite their reports or digests.
- Prioritize **Hero versus non-Hero**, retaining ordinary/ordinary and Hero/Hero.
  Hero status remains intentionally beneficial.
- Preserve meaningful lawful counterplay. Intentional counters may reach
  approximately **90% win rates**; investigate available routes to success when
  defeat would otherwise be certain. This is a design target, not a reported
  win rate or proof that stationary diagnostics model all tactical counterplay.

These standing instructions refine the earlier Upgrade 3 request for extreme
variants: routine specialists must now round out weaknesses. No final balance
winner is approved. Extensive balance studies follow separately after deployment.

## November 23, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED TRAINING ECONOMY AND ASYMMETRIC BALANCE EXPERIMENT (UPGRADE 3)

**Status and precedence:** These are the author's latest explicit redesign
directions for a **separate experimental ruleset**, implemented as `training-1`
with schema 3 in the Combat Lab. They do not replace operative wound combat,
the frozen `hp-d20-1` foundation, working `hp-d20-2`, item catalogues, saved sheets
or existing currency ledgers. Approved directions, approved prototype references,
and unapproved calibration candidates must remain distinguishable.

### Attribute, SP and TP roles

Attributes supply broad, linear power. **Training Points (TP)** buy improvements
to individual derived statistics with escalating costs. **Skill Points (SP)** buy
abilities, Crafts, mundane skills, utility and options, not fundamental statistical
combat power in this experiment. Significant numerical advantages should reflect
significant differences in investment; natural specialization and intensive
training should both matter. All major combat specializations should be capable
of extraordinary performance when heavily invested in.

Retain **two allocatable base attribute points per gained level**, and **+1 to all
five attributes every fifth level**, with existing racial multipliers and lawful
Hero bonuses. Preserve fractional effective attributes until the actual rounding
step. The new five-stat prototype uses STR/DEX/END/INT/Skill; no unapproved global
SPD-to-Skill racial conversion is implied.

Hero bookkeeping remains **2 SP per level**, inclusive of level 1: baseline total
`2×L`. Each SP **earned** also grants **one TP**, creating two separate spendable
pools. Every **allocated attribute point invested in Skill** earns **two extra
SP and two extra TP**. The fifth-level all-stat increase and racial multipliers
are not allocated Skill points and do not independently generate this income.
Track total, spent and unspent TP; reject illegal allocations. Spending SP does
not spend TP and spending TP does not spend SP. Exceptional/racial SP sources
retain their actual provenance and must be declared, not invented.

**Non-Hero advancement is not automatically Hero advancement.** Ordinary mortals
can train attributes under existing canon, but their schedule is unresolved.
The Lab must accept explicit configurable starting attributes, allocated-point
budgets, milestone assumptions, SP/TP supplies and Skill income. Equal-budget
ordinary fixtures are comparison assumptions, not a universal non-Hero leveling
law. Hero status is distinct from Legend or creature classification; do not
grant every Legend a Hero reserve by classification alone.

### Accuracy and evasion: approved candidates for testing

- Physical accuracy: `floor((effective STR + effective DEX)/20) + purchased accuracy`.
- Magical accuracy: `floor((effective INT + effective DEX)/20) + purchased magical accuracy`.
- Evasion: `floor((effective DEX + effective END)/20) + purchased evasion`.

STR and DEX contribute **equally** to physical accuracy. There is **no automatic
flat level bonus**, artificial combined offense/defense cap, +10 attribute ceiling
or former combined permanent proficiency ceiling in this new experiment. Keep
those older limits in their original rulesets. Approximately **+20** is an
ordinary high-level combat benchmark; **+30** indicates extreme specialization,
not a hard ceiling. A high-level character who ignores combat investment may
remain extremely weak. END's combined HP/status-resistance/evasion efficiency
is a balance concern to measure, not authority for an automatic nerf.

The original Upgrade 3 compatibility-default ladder follows. Upgrade 3.1's
newer optional evasion experiment above supersedes evasion prices only when
selected; keep these original prices for backward reproduction and accuracy:


| Purchased ranks | TP per +1 |
|---|---:|
| 1–4 | 2 |
| 5–8 | 4 |
| 9–12 | 7 |
| 13–16 | 11 |
| 17–20 | 16 |

Continue escalating beyond rank 20 through a documented **experimental extension**,
not a hard cap. The Lab's initial extension adds successively larger band-cost
increments, giving 22/29/37/46 TP per rank in the next four-rank bands. This
extension is a configurable-development candidate, **not final numerical canon**.

Support separate training in maximum HP, physical damage, magical damage,
Technique, Movement/Athletics, Initiative/Reflex, armor, magical resistance,
status resistance and other suitable derived statistics. Their prices and units
need not be equal. Compare restrained/balanced/generous effect schedules in a
separate calculation layer. **No finalized universal price list is approved.**

### Damage, durability and defensive identity

Begin with ordinary HP `max(1,floor(50+4×END))`, as an initial reference rather
than an untouchable final calibration. Test smaller **linear** STR damage
coefficients while preserving equal marginal pre-rounding contribution per STR
point. Do not automatically alter existing catalogue WR/F values. A very strong
character with little damage TP should be powerful without routinely one-shotting
equally developed ordinary peers. Eventual one-hit ordinary defeat should generally
need **exceptional offensive attributes and substantial damage training**.

Agile weapons are a high-priority concern because DEX also improves accuracy and
evasion. Compare **50%, 60%, 75% and the full-damage reference**, explicitly
distinguishing reduction of only the DEX contribution from reduction of the
complete ordinary weapon output. Examine subtractive armor and fixed penetration:
halving raw damage can remove far more than half the delivered damage. Preserve
lawful STR-only, DEX-only, Either and Both identities and requirements until their
experimental conversion is explicitly specified.

Extreme HP investment should yield extraordinary durability and aim for comparable
overall effectiveness to extreme evasion, with distinct favorable and unfavorable
matchups. A maximally offensive character should eventually defeat an ordinary
peer in **one successful normal attack**, while an equally developed fully
specialized defensive peer **ordinarily survives**. This is a strong **numerical
goal**, not an immunity or hard damage cap. Test confirmed criticals separately.

Preserve the approved Hero **100%-ordinary-max-HP reserve** and dormant emergency
Break: `ceil(0.51×combined maximum HP)` post-armor damage negates the whole hit,
breaks the Guard and forfeits reserve while retaining preattack ordinary HP.
It cannot trigger once reserve is active. Ordinary HP exhaustion, reserve
activation/depletion, emergency Break, one-hit ordinary exhaustion, critical
one-hit exhaustion and post-Break vulnerability require separate metrics.
Existing rest/lockout and status-Break rulings remain; unresolved overflow and
status-Break timing/action details must be labeled test policies.

### Population priorities and evidence standards

1. **Hero versus non-Hero:** principal intended environment. Being a Hero is
   strictly beneficial; equivalent Hero/ordinary duels are not a 50/50 target.
   Measure the advantage across levels and specializations, and credible threats
   through equipment, attributes, tactics, numbers or counters.
2. **Non-Hero versus non-Hero:** evaluate the underlying economy independently
   of Shimmer, for soldiers, monsters and trained mortals.
3. **Hero versus Hero:** exceptional enemies, bosses and rare PvP. Do not
   sacrifice the principal ordinary environment for perfect Hero-duel symmetry.

Comparable combat builds aspire to average **45–55%** against an explicit common
opponent pool, not every individual matchup. Intentional counters may reach
about **90%**. Identify plausible lawful counterplay for unavoidable defeats
instead of automatically removing the counter. Report per-build, population and
opponent results; symmetric-matrix averages alone are insufficient.

Begin with legal deterministic budget/equipment checks, then controlled fixed-seed,
mirrored matchups at **levels 10, 50 and 100**, including attribute-only, balanced
TP and extreme TP variants. Include STR, trained damage, DEX/evasion, END/HP,
STR/DEX, STR/END, DEX/Skill, INT/Skill, civilians and ordinary combatants, adding
accuracy, armor, status and Technique specialists as supported. Explicitly label
unequal development or unresolved equipment and magic assumptions. Report win
uncertainty, duration, hit probability versus realized hits, attempted/landed
damage, successful hits to defeat, ordinary/reserve absorption, Break, statuses,
budgets, one-hits and impossible/near-impossible routes. Retain machine-readable
inputs, seeds, assumptions and source provenance. Do not canonize a tested winner.

### Technique and incomplete conversions

Investigate a TP-supported **proficiency-scale Technique** separately from the
older raw `max(STR,DEX)` contests. A purchased +1 proficiency is not automatically
one raw Technique point. Preserve operative grapple/disarm/trip/shove, Binding,
Pressure and other established interactions until their conversion is defined.
Retain approved current-ordinary-HP status thresholds and the Hero quarter-max-HP
floor; status resistance must remain meaningful to END specialists. Missing
numerical rules must be exposed as candidates or unsupported mechanics, never
silently invented. This upgrade establishes reusable testing infrastructure;
it does not require or authorize finalizing every subsystem at once.

## November 23, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR CLARIFICATION: CRITICAL CONFIRMATION REUSES PRIMARY ATTACK VALUES (HP/D20)

**APPROVED replacement-prototype clarification, superseding the immediately older “base AC” ambiguity:** If an attack's initial d20 is a **natural 20** and threatens a critical, make a **separate d20 confirmation roll using the EXACT SAME total hit bonus that applied to the primary attack**, including **all temporary and situational attack modifiers then in effect**. Compare its total against the **EXACT SAME defense target (AC) the defender faced for that primary attack**, including all applicable temporary, situational and condition-based changes to that defense. The confirmation uses the primary attack's **snapshotted attack modifier and defense target**, not recalculated, unmodified or "base-only" values. Confirm when `confirmation_d20 + primary_attack_total_hit_bonus >= primary_attack_defense_target` under the previously approved inclusive `>=` hit rule. A successful confirmation delivers the approved **×2 final post-armor damage** (and applicable ×2 status buildup); a failed confirmation delivers **normal landed damage**, not a miss or zero damage. The confirmation is not a new offensive action, does not change the defender's state itself, and does not grant a new attack.

**Clarification of precedent:** In the November 23 predecessor passage below, replace the words “base AC” and its uncertainty about temporary/situational modifiers with **“same actual defense target as the primary attack, using the same hit bonus (including temporaries)”**. That older open question is **RESOLVED** by this latest author statement. The only still-open exceptional attack-roll edge case is whether a natural 20 **automatically hits** when `20 + primary_attack_total_hit_bonus` would otherwise miss; the user has not explicitly changed natural-20 auto-hit behavior. Do not invent an automatic hit by accident. Other approved attack procedures, skill-check criticals and operative wound engine are unaffected.

**Audit calculation, not a simulator result:** For an otherwise ordinary initial natural 20 that meets the primary attack's target, `P(confirmed critical per attempt) = 0.05 × P(d20 + snapshotted hit bonus >= snapshotted defense)`, where the confirmation die is independent. This expression captures temporary attack bonuses and target penalties; it does not assume their absence. Existing connected Combat Lab `hp-d20-1` version 0.8.0 **has not implemented** natural-20 confirmation; any win rates from that version remain old-margin-critical tests until a new version is deployed and verified.

## November 23, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL: NATURAL-20 ATTACK CRITICALS REQUIRE CONFIRMATION (HP/D20)

**Approved new replacement-prototype attack-critical direction, superseding the November 22 margin-based attack-critical trigger only within HP/d20.** An attack's **initial natural d20 roll of 20** creates a potential critical hit. The attacker then **rolls again to confirm** against the defender's **base AC**. **If the confirmation succeeds, resolve the landed attack as a critical hit; if it fails, the attack inflicts ordinary normal damage**, not critical damage. Preserve the previously approved **×2 post-armor HP damage** and applicable **×2 status buildup** for an actually confirmed attack critical. A confirmation is part of the attack's resolution, not a newly purchased offensive action or an additional damaging hit. This **replaces margin >= +10 as the attack-critical success trigger** for the experimental replacement. It does not by itself abolish nonattack d20/skill critical-success/failure categories, change the ordinary Attack/Evasion advancement and SP costs, alter combat-action limits or change operative wound-based combat.

**Base AC / confirmation implementation clarification still OPEN:** The author's term **"base AC"** has not been given an independent unambiguous formal definition in the existing replacement foundation, which uses `d20 + Attack >= 10 + Evasion` for ordinary direct attacks. For a clearly labeled **diagnostic interpretation only**, evaluate confirmation using a new independent d20 plus the normal relevant Attack bonus against `10 + Evasion`; do not canonize that exact interpretation of "base" or assume it ignores/uses special temporary cover, Pressure, aimed-shot penalties, spell modifiers or other adjustments without separate review. The treatment of an initial natural 20 that could not satisfy the ordinary hit target is also **unresolved**; the author's explicit failed-confirmation-normal-damage rule governs an otherwise lawful landed 20 threat. The precise confirmation equality convention, and magical-versus-physical special cases, should use the established general d20 convention only as a test assumption until clarified.

**Audit purpose:** Critical chance is now intended to depend on a natural 20 *and a successful separate confirmation*, so ordinary Attack/Evasion ranks do not automatically raise attack critical probability by five points each near the old margin thresholds. No particular Coordination value, full-build balance result, new Weapon Rating, or final simulator implementation is approved here.

**Existing Combat Lab version note:** Currently connected `hp-d20-1` engine **0.8.0** resolves the prior margin-based attack-critical formula. Previous simulated Coordination win rates remain historical results **under the old critical rule**. New confirmation-critical matchups require a versioned engine update; analytic probability comparisons assuming `10+Evasion` as base AC are not simulations and do not prove resulting full-duel win rates.

## November 23, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL: FULL STR+DEX BOTH-STAT DAMAGE (HP/D20 BATCH 2A); COORDINATION TEST DIRECTION

**Status: approved correction to the EXPERIMENTAL replacement HP/d20 foundation only; operative wound combat remains untouched.** The author corrects the original Batch 1 Both-stat weapon governing contribution from `0.65 × (effective STR + effective DEX)` to **`effective STR + effective DEX`**, retaining full fractional effective attributes until the normal final damage floor. For an eligible Both-stat attack mode, **`raw_damage = floor(effective STR + effective DEX + independent Weapon Rating)`**. The user explicitly requested that STR and DEX each contribute their full values to Both-stat damage; do not retain the 0.65 penalty in later experimental calculations. This replaces the Both formula in the November 22 Batch 1 foundation *for new experimental calculations*. Preserve separately defined STR-only = STR, DEX-only = DEX and Either = highest lawfully eligible STR/DEX, and all legitimate mode requirements. The original November 22 0.65 calculation is retained below as a **superseded historical Batch 1 record**, not an alternative simultaneous rule. The operative wound-engine F and squared-Power mechanics are not modified.

**Recompute fixed mode AP from lawful minimum-stat damage using the corrected full-sum governing attribute**, retaining the existing AP construction rule and Slashing 0%, Blunt 25%, Piercing 50% rates, subtractive armor, post-mitigation critical doubling, and existing rounding/Weight/proficiency rules. Previous Both-stat diagnostic Weapon Ratings and Combat Lab fixtures were calibrated to a different damage equation and are **not** automatic final item ratings or proof of current balance. The Lab's existing `hp-d20-1` worker still implements the earlier 0.65 rule unless independently upgraded and verified; new result sets must explicitly declare the actual executed ruleset/version. Do not silently change its reproducibility benchmarks.

**COORDINATION: AUTHOR-AUTHORIZED EXPERIMENTAL DIRECTION, NOT A FINAL APPROVED BONUS.** Test eligibility where `min(effective STR, effective DEX) >= 0.90 × max(effective STR, effective DEX)`. Explore **+1, +2, +3 and +4** candidate Coordination bonuses. The intended tactical option is to **choose at the beginning of each of the eligible character's turns** whether that turn's entire candidate bonus benefits **Attack OR Evasion**, never both or a split allocation. The author's desired niche is a martial hybrid's deliberate turn-by-turn tactical flexibility. The final value, adaptive decision policy, exact application/reassessment of attribute-based eligibility, initial stance, whether the bonus is treated as temporary for permanent proficiency ceilings, and interaction with tactical actions/other modifiers remain **OPEN for testing and explicit approval**. Fixed Attack-only and Evasion-only temporary-modifier comparisons in the current Lab do not simulate active turn-by-turn switching.

**Next step:** compare full-sum baseline, +1/+2/+3/+4 static allocations and dynamic turn-start stance choices, including equal-investment STR/DEX builds, later tactical attacks and real equipment loadouts. Preserve operative equipment, material, Quality, SP/MP and cost identities until separately authorized conversion.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED BATCH 1 LINEAR HP/D20 EXPERIMENTAL FOUNDATION

**Status: approved experimental replacement-prototype canon.** This foundation is authorized for Combat Lab HP/d20 testing. It does **not** replace the operative wound-based engine, historical weapon F ratings, squared Power/Defense, wound resolution, armor/shield catalogues, materials, enchantments, Craft costs, SP/MP rules, pricing or live campaign sheets. Earlier prototype notes leaving these exact Batch 1 equations or rounding open are superseded **only within this experimental foundation**.

### Calculation order and boundaries

1. Retain fractional effective attributes through applicable racial and other lawful modifiers; preserve the existing hard minimum of 1 for effective attributes. Do not prematurely round attribute contributions.
2. Ordinary maximum HP: `max(1, floor(50 + 4 × effective END))`.
3. Conventional melee governing contribution `A`: STR = `effective STR`; DEX = `effective DEX`; Either = `max(effective STR, effective DEX)` subject to legitimate equipment requirements; Both = `0.65 × (effective STR + effective DEX)`, requiring both equipment minima.
4. Normal unmodified raw damage: `floor(A + independent Weapon Rating)`. Weapon Rating is an independent profile value, not legacy F, a squared rating, or a universal handling-class bonus.
5. Each legitimate attack mode stores its own fixed AP: `minimum_stat_damage = floor(A_at_minimum_legal_equipment_stats + Weapon Rating)`; `AP = floor(minimum_stat_damage × penetration_rate)`. Rates: Slashing **0%**, Blunt **25%**, Piercing **50%**. Either uses the **lowest lawful qualifying baseline configuration**, retaining DEX-mode support-STR gates; Both requires both minima. Do not promote provisional Light/Medium minima to final canon.
6. AP is calculated once per actual constructed weapon/profile/mode. Actual approved material/Quality construction may require recalculating that profile's AP; increasing wielder attributes or temporary combat damage bonuses does **not** change AP. No new material/Quality conversion is approved here.
7. `effective_armor = max(0, applicable armor − fixed AP)`; `net_HP_damage = max(0, final_raw_damage − effective_armor)`. Armor is subtractive; penetration reduces armor, never increases raw damage. Fully negated attacks do zero damage, without automatic chip damage.
8. Critical attacks double **actual post-mitigation HP damage**. Do not double pre-armor damage.
9. Shared equipped Weight capacity: `floor(effective STR + effective END)`. Declare real equipped-item weights; baseline tests reject insufficient attributes or overweight fixtures. Missing catalogue weights and encumbrance penalties remain unresolved.
10. Round down to whole numbers **after each complete relevant HP, damage, AP or Weight calculation**. Preserve fractions until each such calculation is complete.

### Preserved approved martial d20 and proficiency rules

Direct hit: `d20 + Attack >= 10 + Evasion`. Margin **>= +10** is critical success; margin **<= −10** is critical failure. Natural 1/20 are not automatically critical. Martial Attack attribute bonus is `min(10, floor((STR + DEX/2)/20))`; Evasion attribute bonus is `min(10, floor((END + DEX/2)/20))`. Legal purchased ranks are separate, at most **10**, with combined permanent attribute+purchased proficiency ceiling `min(20, 10 + floor(level/10))`. Temporary modifiers remain separate from that ceiling. Preserve **two actions per turn and at most one offensive attack**. This foundation supplies no new permanent initiative rule and no numerical HP conversion for Power Attack, Double Strike or other undefined maneuvers.

### Default generalist calibration reference

The default physical test reference is a **medium-armored, physically neutral Untouched Serpentari**, base STR **17**, DEX **16**, END **17**, with no equipment stat bonuses. Allocate **one point to STR and one to END per gained level**, plus the existing **+1 to all attributes every fifth level**. Thus STR and END are `17 + (level−1) + floor(level/5)`; DEX is `16 + floor(level/5)`. This does not assign an unapproved SKILL racial modifier, spend SP or assume purchased ranks.

| Level | STR | END | Ordinary max HP | Weight capacity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | 17 | 17 | 118 | 34 |
| 10 | 28 | 28 | 162 | 56 |
| 25 | 46 | 46 | 234 | 92 |
| 50 | 76 | 76 | 354 | 152 |
| 75 | 106 | 106 | 474 | 212 |
| 100 | 136 | 136 | 594 | 272 |

Existing armor calibration anchors remain:

| Progression reference | Light armor / Weight | Medium armor / Weight | Heavy armor / Weight |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 10 | 10 / 6 | 20 / 12 | 35 / 22 |
| 50 | 40 / 20 | 75 / 35 | 110 / 55 |
| 100 | 65 / 35 | 115 / 55 | 165 / 85 |

These anchors are **calibration fixtures**, not item level requirements, completed material profiles or permission to interpolate missing levels. Medium armor is the reference category; no missing item weight or exact armor at L1/25/75 is invented.

**Provisional balance objective:** an equally developed medium-armored generalist should ordinarily exhaust ordinary HP after **4–6 successful normal attacks**. This is a design target, not a measured result or guarantee. HP reaching zero is an **HP exhaustion test endpoint**, without a new ruling on incapacitation, death, injury, Shimmer activation or Legend behavior.

**Diagnostic fixture, not finalized catalogue conversion:** STR-scaling test warhammer, minimum STR 11, WR 77: baseline damage 88, fixed Blunt AP 22; STR136 gives raw213, damage120 through armor115, and five normal landed attacks to exhaust HP594. This does not canonize the provisional Medium warhammer minimum or WR as a final item profile.

**Still unresolved / outside Batch 1:** complete Weapon Rating and Weight catalogues, material/Quality conversions, shields/Guard/Parry/overflow, Shimmer/Break/healing/death/Legend, spells/statuses/MR, ranged combat, tactics/positioning/Pressure, structural/object/enchantment conversion, undefined attack maneuvers, full equipment/wealth validation and permanent initiative integration. Preserve all separately approved prototype directions and operative systems until their own conversions are approved.

## November 22, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION: STRUCTURAL BREACH 25%/50%, LOCALIZED DAMAGE AND MAGNITUDE

**HP/d20 replacement prototype, superseding the previously documented 10%/25% breach thresholds:** A **passable hole**, large enough for **one creature/person to get through**, requires **at least 25% of the targeted wall section's maximum HP dealt as actual damage after armor**. A **large hole**, allowing **multiple people to squeeze through**, requires **at least 50%**. These are local section thresholds, not a claim that one attack damages the entire structure. The baseline target without sufficient Magnitude is **a relatively small local wall section**, while **the rest of the wall or castle remains largely intact**. To damage a **larger wall area, an entire castle, or other extensive structure at once**, an attack must have **sufficient lawful Magnitude/area coverage**. Do not treat point-targeted Power as automatic unlimited area. If the targeted small section is **totally destroyed**, the remaining connected wall **might lose about 10% of its own HP** as collateral structural weakening, **conditionally** where the actual construction/support relationship justifies it; this is not an automatic guaranteed 10% and should not be applied blindly to an entire castle. Exact wall-section footprint/HP allocation, local-vs-global propagation rules, damage accumulation across hits, and Magnitude category boundaries remain unresolved.

Preserve the five already-approved material-equivalent Armor references (thin wood L1, ordinary wood L10, thin stone L25, thick stone L75, steel L100), based on an END specialist with their permitted medium-armor Weight entirely invested in armor. No actual wall maximum HP has yet been approved. This supersedes former “10% hole/25% large hole” for the **replacement prototype only**, not a silent overwrite of the operative wound/structure system.

## November 22, 2026 — APPROVED SHIELDS, WALL ARMOR AND BREACH DAMAGE (HP/D20 REPLACEMENT PROTOTYPE)

**Shield benchmark:** Against same-level generalist fighter maximum **ordinary** HP `H_G(L)`, Light and Medium shield each have **H_G(L)/2 HP**, while Heavy shield has **2× that half = H_G(L) HP**. Light shield armor is **light armor equivalent**; Medium is **the generalist fighter's armor equivalent** (medium reference pending definition); Heavy is **heavy armor equivalent**. The user's wording “light armor” is read as “light shield” based on context and must not be silently converted to 1/4 HP. Exact reference builds and HP rounding OPEN; all existing material and Guard exceptions preserved.

**Structures:** Wall armor should correspond to **an Endurance specialist wearing medium armor and allocating their entire eligible equipment Weight budget to armor**, at the indicated progression level. Five author-provided reference wall categories: **thin wood level 1; normal wood level 10; thin stone level 25; thick stone level 75; steel level 100**. The author also names a **normal castle wall as the baseline**, but its exact placement among these five categories, HP and thickness are NOT separately specified. **At least 10% of the structure's maximum HP in actual net damage creates a hole; at least 25% creates a large hole**, subject to legal attack coverage and localization. Eligible attack damage must first be reduced by wall armor as approved in the HP prototype; do not confuse raw attack Power with net HP damage. These thresholds describe **damage needed for a hole**, not automatic subtraction of 10%/25% HP for an intact adjacent tile, nor proof any arbitrary-area attack can open an entire wall. The time window / whether successive hits can accumulate toward one hole, footprint and whether wall HP is per panel/tile remain OPEN.

**Scope:** Approved HP/d20 replacement-prototype baselines, not yet a rewritten operative wound/structure/shield engine, material catalogue, equipment prices or any simulation result. See `29_Physical_Shield_Profiles.md` and `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md`.

## November 22, 2026 — BURNING RESET, SHIMMER HP ABSORPTION AND TILE WATER CONTACT (APPROVED PROTOTYPE)

**New author approval:** Burning tick = **ceil(maximum ordinary HP / 3)**, **Shimmer Guard HP excluded from the damage-amount basis but INCLUDED as normal damage-absorbing protection** whenever the Shimmer reserve is active and legally available. Apply standard ordinary-HP/Shimmer HP routing; Burning is not a special Shimmer-bypassing damage type. **When advanced Burning II is reduced to standard Burning I**, **restart the Severity I end-of-victim-turn damage cadence as though Burning had just been applied**: next tick at end of victim's next proper turn after downgrade; subsequent Severity I ticks every other victim turn, if still active. Do not keep the imminent Severity II tick. The status buildup is NOT erased or refreshed solely by restarting the damage clock. Timing for I→II upgrade remains open.

**Terrain conversion:** **One puddle can extinguish one fire tile**, transforming that fire to **cold ash** and the puddle's water to **steam**, consistent with existing water/fire exclusivity. This consumes that puddle's one-tile extinguishing contribution; do not spread its benefit across extra fire tiles automatically. Ice meeting fire produces **water and cold ash**, under its own approved contact reaction, without invented cascading heat effects. See `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md` for status/media rules. The old wound engine is not converted by this prototype decision.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: ROUND UP BURNING; FIRST TICK ON VICTIM TURN; TERRAIN EXCLUSIONS

In the developing HP/d20 prototype, Burning tick damage is **ceil(maximum ordinary HP / 3)**, counting no Shimmer Guard HP in the maximum-HP calculation. **First tick occurs at the END of the victim's next proper turn** after Burning takes effect. Subsequent ticks are at the end of **every other** victim turn for Severity I and **every** victim turn for Severity II. Victim may legally extinguish before end-turn damage when able. Whether actual HP damage is absorbed by Shimmer HP is still open, as are timing when Burning begins on the victim's active turn and phase changes when severity changes.

**Terrain:** Magical fire and Hex/threads may coexist. Fire and **hot ash cannot coexist** on one tile; fire and **water/puddles/ice cannot coexist**. Fire contacting water/puddles transforms **fire → cold ash**, **water → steam**. Fire contacting ice transforms **fire → cold ash**, **ice → water**. Cold ash does not count as hot ash for the established 0.5× environmental Burning buildup. Existing 1× fire, 1× Hex, 0.5× hot-ash buildup rates and once-per-status-per-turn exposure remain. Terrain extinguishing is not automatically the same as immediately curing an already Burning creature. Source `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: EXACT 1/3 BURNING TICK AND PER-TURN ENVIRONMENTAL BUILDUP

**Burning supersession:** Replace **33%** with **exactly one-third (1/3) of the victim's maximum ordinary HP per Burning tick** in the HP/d20 prototype. Severity I ticks at the END of every OTHER victim turn; Severity II ticks at the END of EVERY victim turn. Shimmer Guard does not enlarge the base for calculating one-third. Damage rounding, tick phase, and absorption through active Shimmer HP remain OPEN.

**Environmental exposure:** Standing in magical **fire** adds **1× the caster's offensive Spell Power** to Burning buildup; standing in magical **Hex** adds **1× offensive Spell Power** to Hex buildup; standing on **hot ash** adds **0.5× offensive Spell Power** to Burning buildup. Running/traversing multiple affected tiles on the same turn does not add repeated buildup for that same status. **One environmental application per affected status per creature turn**, not per tile. Distinct Fire and Hex types may be tracked independently. These exposures do not create new direct HP ticks beyond separately lawful damage, including regular Burning's end-of-turn ticks. Overlap/timing/source attribution still need review. Reference `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md` for owning universal status framework.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED BURNING TICK AND BINDING PART PERSISTENCE (HP/D20 PROTOTYPE)

**Burning:** Severity I inflicts **33% of the victim's maximum ordinary HP** as damage **at the end of every other victim turn**; Severity II deals the SAME **33% of maximum ordinary HP** **at the end of every victim turn**. Shimmer Guard is **not counted toward the max-HP damage amount**; actual Shimmer absorption/bypass is not newly decided. Valid extinguishing on the victim's turn before the end-of-turn tick can prevent it. First tick, partial-clock handling on severity changes, exact 33% rounding and exact relationship to Shimmer reserve remain OPEN. Do not infer natural ongoing damage automatically retriggers a new status severity evaluation. Burning can still be fully extinguished by a lawful named complete-removal counter.

**Binding:** A Jester's selected one part (arm, leg, mouth, eyes) **cannot change while that same Binding status persists**. Complete Severity II binds whole body; a reduction back to Severity I restores restraint on the original selected part. The Jester may choose a new part only for a genuinely new Binding after the prior one has ended. These are approved replacement-prototype rules; old operative rules and specialist exceptions remain separate. Owning status `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`; Jester `05_Jestercraft.md`.

---

## November 22, 2026 — APPROVED BINDING TIER ACTION RESTRICTIONS (REPLACEMENT PROTOTYPE)

**Severity I:** one Jester-chosen part (arm, leg, mouth or eyes) bound with its approved functional effect; no blanket full-body incapacity. **Severity II (advanced):** whole body is bound; **only self-directed escape attempts are allowed**. The victim cannot otherwise attack, cast spells, take normal movement or perform unrelated actions while fully bound; outside ally rescues remain valid. Lawful reduction below active Severity II downgrades immediately to one-part Severity I, and below Severity I releases them. This is a specific restriction within the general status engine and does not grant a free escape action, override costs/checks or collapse Jestercraft Binding into separate Thread Grappling. See `05_Jestercraft.md` and `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`. Older notes contemplating binding multiple parts cumulatively are superseded within the replacement prototype.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED BINDING TARGET CHOICE AND BLIND ATTACKS (HP/D20 PROTOTYPE)

**Binding targeting:** The attacking Jester chooses the selected arm, leg, mouth or eyes when a lawful standard Binding is applied. A bound **mouth silences** the target; block mouth-/speech-dependent actions (including only genuinely verbal spells), not all magic by default. Bound **eyes blind**, so the victim **cannot visually detect targets**. A blind attacker who correctly guesses or deduces an enemy's tile may attack that tile if otherwise legally targetable, with **−4 to Attack accuracy**. A correct tile does not restore sight; wrong tile does not automatically redirect the attack. Alternative sensory detection and precise target requirements remain governed by specific abilities and are not waived. This −4 is an **accuracy** penalty, separate from Pressure's **−2 per net PP to Evasion and Reflex checks** (max −6 at 3 PP, when Trapped occurs). The Jester's choice does not grant multiple bound body parts from one standard application; stacking/multiple applications remain OPEN. Other approved Binding constraints and legacy operative engine remain intact. Owning Jestercraft file: `05_Jestercraft.md`.

---

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: FREEZE/BINDING AND PRESSURE-REFLEX INTERACTIONS (PROTOTYPE)

**Standard Freeze:** movement speed **halved**; grants **two Pressure Points** against affected creature. **Standard Binding:** disables one actual limb, treating it as functionally absent. Bound arm cannot use a spellcasting implement requiring it, use an arm-mounted shield, or help wield a two-handed weapon. Bound leg **halves movement speed and gives one Pressure Point**. In both cases ordinary net-pressure rules, meaningful counter-pressure and three-point cap apply. **Every effective PP reduces Evasion AND Reflex checks by 2** (1/2/3+ => −2/−4/−6); three net PP causes Trapped in this replacement prototype, with eligible automatic/passive shield blocks continuing. Advanced Freeze and Binding retain their complete incapacitation and ordinary counterplay methods. Limb targeting, multiple-limb stacking and movement rounding are not newly approved. Owning unified status rules: `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`; Jestercraft specifics: `05_Jestercraft.md`. **This is approved replacement-prototype canon, not a global operative-engine conversion.**

## November 22, 2026 — UNIFIED STATUS RESOLUTION CROSS-REFERENCE (APPROVED PROTOTYPE)

The author approved the **unified status engine** as the default for new conditions and the organized migration of existing statuses in the HP/d20 replacement prototype. See [Magical Media: unified status engine](35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md) for the authoritative definition template, delivery/threshold/severity/reduction/duration ordering, named exceptions, and open implementation issues. Core rules: standard 1× effective status HP, advanced 2×, 1× damage+1× buildup hybrid or 3× pure buildup, ×0.5 status Power for advanced-incapacitating effects, critical ×2 buildup, 3-round refreshed accumulation, no spontaneous severity escalation when HP falls, ordinary ally counter 0.5× standard or 0.25× advanced (special full clears possible), immediate downgrade/removal from lawful reduction. Distinguish active-duration timers from buildup expiration. No new simulator test or operative wound-engine migration is implied.

## November 22, 2026 — APPROVED REPLACEMENT-PROTOTYPE STATUS AND D20 CONVERSION (NOT YET OPERATIVE)

**Authority:** Latest author-approved replacement-prototype direction. These rules supersede conflicting older rules **within the developing HP/d20 prototype only**. Do not globally rewrite the operative quadratic/wound/typed-SR engine, existing Craft prerequisites, source geometry, exceptional spell effects, or item statistics before integrated migration.

**General d20:** Everyone begins with **zero purchased proficiency ranks**, earns all purchases; attribute-derived bonuses remain separate. Purchased Attack improves BOTH martial and magical attacks, and Evasion defends against both. Magical Attack's attribute association is **INT + half DEX**, martial Attack **STR + half DEX**, Evasion **END + half DEX**. Rename Movement to **Athletics** (movement speed plus climbing, swimming, acrobatics, sports), and Initiative to **Reflex** (rolled for combat initiative plus reactions and qualifying AoE avoidance). Proficiency checks: `d20 + proficiency >= DC` with ordinary DCs 5–35; margin >= +10 is critical success, <= −10 is critical failure, natural 1/20 have no automatic critical meaning. Direct attacks hit when `d20 + Attack >= 10 + Evasion`; AoE escape succeeds when `d20 + Reflex >= 10 + spell Attack`. **Critical attack = ×2 HP damage AND ×2 status buildup** (including temporary Attack/Evasion modifiers influencing critical probability). Specific AoE half-damage-on-success remains attack-specific and OPEN. Retain existing two-action/one-offensive-attack allowance.

**Pressure:** Each net Pressure Point (PP), up to three, penalizes BOTH Evasion and Reflex by **−2**, for −2/−4/−6 at 1/2/3+ PP. **At 3 net PP, Trapped occurs automatically**, overriding the older independent-Trapped distinction in this prototype. Trapped prevents ordinary defensive actions from going off, but **eligible passive/automatic shield blocks remain possible**, along with actual armor/barrier protection; reconcile older Guard restrictions at extreme PP in the integrated migration. Existing credible-source, counter-pressure, and action-economy rules remain where compatible.

**HP-based status engine:** Status thresholds use **current ordinary HP**, not a separate Status Resistance statistic. For Heroes, use at least **25% of their maximum ordinary HP** as effective status HP: `H_status=max(current ordinary HP, 0.25×max ordinary HP)`. General standard severity = **1× H_status**, advanced severity = **2× H_status** (working author-selected benchmark for this prototype). A landed damaging status spell delivers **1× lawful Spell Power in direct pre-mitigation damage and 1× in buildup**; pure status spells deliver **3× lawful Spell Power in buildup and no direct HP damage**. Spells whose effect **incapacitates at advanced severity** have **half outgoing offensive Power for status application before their eligible buildup multiplier**. Such hostile applications are attacks and must hit legally. Ordinary MR should **not automatically reduce status buildup** in the prototype test baseline, but special defenses, actual intercepted contact, and material exceptions still require testing and specific conversion; retain legacy magic/counter-material mechanics until conversion. No universal change to independent elemental-mode exceptions (Blue/White Flame etc.) or named Craft special forms is automatically implied.

**Timing and expiry:** Eligible new status applications add buildup and refresh the existing **three-round buildup window**. A newly applied status checks its severity against the target's then-current effective status HP **when the status application lands**; ordinary damage or ongoing Burning lowering HP does **not** independently trigger a new status/severity. Exact pre-hit versus post-hit HP evaluation for a simultaneous damaging status spell remains OPEN for final adjudication. Existing active statuses are downgraded as buildup is legitimately removed below their active threshold and end when reduced below minimum threshold, subject to specific counter exceptions. Many advanced immobilizations have no fixed short duration and need removal; Sleep's advanced unconsciousness lasts **one minute** (unless awakened sooner); actual ice may naturally melt over many minutes.

**Ordinary allied counters:** Where a named condition has a legitimate ordinary nonmagical rescue/counter, typical successful allied intervention reduces buildup by **0.5× current effective status HP for a standard status** or **0.25× for an advanced status** per applicable action, subject to specific status exceptions, action permissions, and legal targeting. One ally should generally slow, not completely neutralize, a four-hit incapacitating caster; multiple committed allies can counteract it. Some physical/environmental counters remove **all** buildup, e.g. successfully drenching/extinguishing an ordinary Burning victim with sufficient water. The special full extinguish is NOT a generic 0.5× reduction. Wakecraft Cleanse and Warlock Dispel remain distinct magical counters; the prior Encourage **INT² / INT²÷2** rule is operative historical canon but is **superseded for the new replacement prototype** by the approved proportional counter direction, pending exact one-/two-action mapping and relationship qualifications.

**Status-specific migration ledger:**
- **Sleep:** Severity I/ordinary Sleep imposes **−2 AC and −2 on all d20 checks**, but does **not** incapacitate. Advanced Sleep renders victim unconscious for **one minute**; physical shaking or damaging the already-sleeping person awakens them. A conscious isolated Sleep target has **no ordinary physical buildup-reduction/self-cure**; allies may wake them once they sleep. Terminology note: the author's phrase “Sleep 3” refers to the full incapacitating outcome, but the global new model uses two severity thresholds; exact tier naming remains to be reconciled without inventing a third status tier.
- **Charm:** Lesser Charm retains friendlier/suggestible behavior and established charmer-specific attacks penalty where compatible; **advanced Charm** alone causes side-switch/incapacitating agency loss. An isolated charmed target has no ordinary self-counter. Valid relationship-based **Encourage** from another person counters psychic status (exact per-action percentage mapping versus former INT² remains an integration detail). Preserve prior triggering-attack breaks-Charm-after-resolution and existing duration until individually revised.
- **Freeze:** Basic Freeze may hinder movement/control without total immobilization; fully immobilizing/incapacitating Freeze is **advanced** and takes the halved incapacitating offensive Power where relevant. Damage/break the actual ice to reduce/remove the condition; physical ice may melt naturally over a longer period. Exact damage-to-buildup conversion, Shatter and specialized Wakecraft deliveries require subject-specific calibration.
- **Jestercraft Binding:** Lesser Binding restricts without complete incapacitation; complete helplessness is **advanced**, with the new halved-incapacitating status offense modifier. Victims/allies can destroy/damage emitted restraints; cutting one captive free does not erase the reusable Draped source. Binding remains an attack, with normal mana cost/source/range and separate captives. Distinguish its **underlying ordinary spell Power calculation** from its **outgoing effective status potency after the new incapacitation modifier**, to reconcile old “full Binding Power” source language.
- **Living Alchemy Polymorph/Transmogrified:** Full helpless living transformation is advanced; ordinary rescues may **heal/remove the sigil scar**, subject to actual healing/action/Toxicity requirements. Earlier specialty `2×` Transmogrified buildup and old `4×` threshold must be reconciled with the general pure-status 3× and advanced 2× conversion; do NOT silently stack multipliers or remove the special living-target prerequisites, anchoring, six-second reformation or physical-loss rules.
- **Burning:** Its ordinary flaming status can be extinguished completely by a legitimate physical/environmental action, such as sufficient water, rather than requiring stepwise percentage reduction. Preserve distinct Smoldering/Burning/Engulfed identities and three-flame mode exceptions; precise translation of old 0.5×/1×/4× fire thresholds to the new standard/advanced ladder is OPEN rather than inventing extra effects.
- **Other psychic effects:** Existing nonmagical **Encourage** and named relationship/range/hearing constraints survive in principle. Clarify which emotional controls are incapacitating under the new advanced-only principle; don't silently rewrite their bespoke conditions.

**Hero emergency Break vs statuses:** Shimmer reserve does NOT ordinarily resist buildup. If a status would defeat/incapacitate a Hero and Shimmer Guard is **still dormant**, the Hero can break that Guard to reduce the defeating status to **1× effective status HP**; it cannot be used once the Guard has begun bolstering HP. The precise choice/timing/action requirement remains OPEN. This complements, not replaces, the separately approved emergency high-damage Shimmer Break.

**Benchmark and testing:** Aim, against equal-Power unprotected ordinary-HP targets, for direct damage to win after ~**3 successful hits**, advanced pure-status attacks after ~**4 successful hits**; with meaningful MR, direct damage after ~**5–6 successful hits** while status remains ~4 when no specific status defense applies. Example 300 HP and base spell Power 100: pure incapacitating status = `100×0.5×3=150` buildup per hit, advanced threshold `2×300=600`, four successful status hits; damage gives 100 per hit before MR, three hits unprotected, five or six hits under illustrative 40–50% mitigation. **Not** guaranteed turns, win rates or a Combat Lab run. Historical anti-magic material matchups remain calibration goals, not proof under the new engine.

**Open conversion:** Reconcile older two-tier and 4× severity terminology, named status break/removal exceptions, physical damage and status ordering, original Jester Binding-Power specialty, Nymbrin double-buildup exception, physical-armor/MR intercept, automatic shield coverage at 3 PP, exact rounding and duration, no-sleep-self-cleanse boundaries, and implementation in Combat Lab. All preexisting Craft/racial/operative mechanics continue until full migration.

## November 22, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL: Skill-based progression prices, Dexterity proficiencies, movement ladder and LEVEL-BASED SP (REPLACEMENT PROTOTYPE)

**Authority/scope:** The author expressly approves this new Skill-attribute/proficiency direction for the planned holistic combat/character-system replacement. It is **approved prototype canon**, not permission to silently replace the existing five-stat, quest-awarded-SP, wound-based operative engine across other subject files. Retain preexisting subject-specific Craft SP prices, gates, racial starting entitlements, and special effects until faithful integrated migration. This author approval supersedes the earlier assistant-only pricing proposals and former SP-for-quests expectation **within the new prototype**.

**Five-attribute redesign direction:** Replace Speed (SPD) with a **SKILL** attribute that contributes additional spendable **Skill Points (SP)** and improves relevant Handling under the emerging design. Movement and Initiative are **separate proficiencies**, both **associated with DEX**, not direct SPD-ratio calculations. Their attribute bonus is to follow the shared per-20-qualifying-attribute-point approach; exact starting negative-stat handling, rounding and universal proficiency associations need final implementation checks.

**Approved SP supply:** Heroes receive **2 SP per level** as baseline level-tied income (for bookkeeping, test a total of `2×level` SP by level L, inclusive of level 1), **plus 2 additional SP for every one allocated point placed in the SKILL attribute**. This converts progression away from requiring **divine quest rewards to grant ordinary SP**. Existing patron XP and Gem reward systems are not thereby abolished; any exceptional SP rewards, refund/respec rules, retroactive skill conversion, racial freebies and granting pace require an explicit migration decision. Crucially, attribute allocation points and SP are distinct currencies: one allocated point in SKILL generates 2 spendable SP, not two free proficiency ranks.

**Approved purchase prices:** Accuracy **5 SP per purchased rank**, Evasion **5 SP**, Technique **3 SP**, Movement **3 SP**, Initiative **3 SP**, and mundane proficiency such as Lockpicking **2 SP per purchased rank**. Flat per-rank price, with **no escalating price for later ranks**. Existing supernatural Craft upgrade SP prices remain as separately approved (normally **5 SP** for Warlock/Wakecraft/Vren rank; Jestercraft has legitimate **3-SP modifiers and 4-SP applications**). No automatic wholesale repricing of named Crafts. Under the current redesign direction a proficiency can buy **at most 10 permanent ranks**; supernatural Craft disciplines with distinct existing five-rank trees are NOT silently changed to ten ranks.

**Approved relevant attribute association:** Accuracy derives from full STR plus half DEX; Evasion from full END plus half DEX; **Technique gets full STR and full DEX**, Movement and Initiative are associated with DEX. The developing general attribute bonus rule is **+1 per complete 20 relevant attribute points**, provisionally `floor(relevant attribute sum/20)`; attribute-derived Accuracy and Evasion remain **capped at +10** as previously approved. Applying this same +10 contribution ceiling to other proficiencies is a conversion candidate, not permission to overwrite their special rules before review.

**Permanent proficiency ceiling:** The author approves the assistant's recommendation to use **combined purchased + attribute-derived permanent proficiency maximum `min(20,10+floor(level/10))`**, with maximum permanent **+20**, separate from temporary spells, blessings, conditions and situational adjustments that may exceed it. The 10-purchased-rank constraint remains separate. Whether purchases above the currently usable level ceiling can be prepaid and retained as inactive ranks remains a suggested convenience, not specifically approved.

**Approved movement scaling (new prototype):** Base movement **30 ft** (6 five-foot tiles), increased **5 ft / one tile for every 2 points of effective Movement proficiency**, to **80 ft maximum normal permanent movement** (at effective Movement proficiency 20). Formula candidate `min(80,30+5×floor(Movement proficiency/2))` ft. One Dash doubles the calculated ordinary allowance; Double Dash doubles again (2× / 4× base). A Double Dash retreat ignores the first tile for hostile Zone-of-Control purposes as separately approved. Temporary magical movement exceptions above the permanent cap and precise handling of negative Movement values remain open.

**Approved magic-skill direction:** Magical actions may use relevant **magical Skill checks**, subject to the Craft's own learned ranks, INT/other attribute prerequisites, Channel Limit, MP, medium and resistance rules; no universal new spell-hit target or magic contest formula is approved merely from this direction. Advanced supernatural effects require appropriate intellectual/craft prerequisites; difficult mundane tasks may require multiple abilities such as DEX plus INT.

**Related, still to settle:** Whether magic attacks buy ordinary Accuracy or a distinct magical proficiency; base attack check and d20 natural extremes; default starting Accuracy/Evasion; initiative proficiency's actual turn-order procedure; exact race/monster starting SKILL and free ranks; whether movement/initiative have independent flat +1 for each 20 DEX or share a cap; magic contests, status checks, Technique contests and armor interaction; precise SP per-level award timing at first level; how SKILL attribute interacts with the approved five-year racial rebase/stat milestones. **No tests or simulator runs claimed.**

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED HP-PROTOTYPE ACCURACY/EVASION ATTRIBUTE BONUS CEILING (+10)

**Approved selection:** For the developing SKILL/proficiency-based combat replacement, select **Option B**, capping **permanent attribute-derived Accuracy and Evasion contributions at +10 each**. This selects the previously offered +10 attribute-bonus ceiling rather than unlimited attribute scaling. The working associated-attribute candidate remains `Accuracy attribute bonus = min(10, floor((STR + DEX/2)/20))` and `Evasion attribute bonus = min(10, floor((END + DEX/2)/20))`; the author described one bonus per 20 points, with half of DEX applied to each. Handling of negative attributes, floors, and attribute rebasing requires holistic conversion. This approval does NOT automatically approve a universal combined-skill-score cap at 20 or particular purchased-rank SP costs.

**NEW USER-DIRECTED CAP CANDIDATE, NOT YET FINALIZED:** The author proposes that the **maximum permanent skill bonus** is `min(20, 10 + floor(character level/10))`, making the ceiling +10 at level 1, +11 at level 10, +15 at level 50, and +20 at level 100. The proposed interpretation is a cap on the **combined permanent rank bonus** (purchased ranks + attribute-derived bonus), separate from transient magical/situational modifiers; confirm the precise meaning before migration. Keep the previously discussed **at most ten purchased ranks in each proficiency** as the working design direction. No new automatic free ranks or SP budget are approved here.

**PRICING UNDER REVIEW ONLY:** Existing canon Warlock, Wakecraft, Vren and similar supernatural Craft ranks generally cost **5 SP per rank**, whereas Jestercraft modifiers/apps are **3/4 SP**; none are automatically repriced. Test **5 SP per purchased Accuracy rank, 5 SP per Evasion rank**, and substantially cheaper mundane proficiency ranks (a possible recurring **1,2,2 SP** purchase schedule, 3 ranks for 5 SP), with Movement/Initiative prices unapproved pending their effects. Regular hero level/SP grants and the proposed SKILL-attribute-to-SP conversion remain open. These costs are assistant proposals, NOT author approvals or current shop prices.

**Implementation boundary:** Operative SPD/Combat Speed/wound rules continue until the comprehensive redesign is formally authorized. Preserve racial and Craft-specific free SP/abilities, attribute requirements, training exceptions, and existing XP/SP distinction.

## November 22, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL: movement and initiative as distinct Proficiencies; 30-foot base and Double Dash escape (REPLACEMENT PROTOTYPE)

**Approval scope:** Within the planned additive-HP / Skill-based-combat redesign, the author approves replacing the SPD attribute's movement/initiative responsibilities with **separate purchasable Movement and Initiative Proficiencies**, allowing distinct character build investment. Exact skill costs, proficiency progression, numerical effects on movement, combat initiative ordering and treatment of monster/racial exceptional speed remain OPEN. The larger replacement of SPD with a SKILL attribute, Weapon Skill/Evasion proficiency scale and d20 target number are still under design and **not silently adopted as operative canon**.

**Movement baseline:** Base movement is **30 feet** (six 5-foot tiles). **Each Dash doubles the base movement allowance sequentially:** one Dash allows **60 feet / 12 tiles**, and two Dashes (Double Dash) allow **120 feet / 24 tiles**. Treat Dash as the existing one-action commitment and Double Dash as two actions for the prototype, subject to later action-economy integration; no extra independent attack is granted. This **supersedes the old 1.5× / 2× Dash movement multiplier within the proposed replacement ruleset only**. Existing operative pilot values remain unchanged until full conversion. Movement Proficiency bonuses and whether they modify the baseline before doubling remain open.

**Double Dash zone-of-control escape:** A character committing to **Double Dash ignores the first movement tile for purposes of hostile zones of control** when departing/retreating; this permits a lawful retreat from the immediate controlling space without the otherwise relevant initial ZOC contest. This does **not** automatically ignore later controlled tiles, real physical obstacles, bindings, walls, protected reach geometry or other independent restrictions. Exact timing/interaction with multiple overlapping zones, reach, tactical exceptions and Initiative remains to be finalized.

**Baseline accuracy/evasion direction (approved concept, UNAPPROVED numbers):** The author favors modest free or automatically earned starting Weapon Skill / Evasion competence rather than making every introductory combat rank a player purchase. Candidate distribution approaches include small starting ranks, infrequent fixed automatic ranks, or occasional free ranks allocated by the player between Weapon Skill and Evasion. No starting score, level cadence, 1–20 rank purchase prices, natural-d20 rule, automatic award budget or rate of SKILL-to-SP exchange is authorized by this decision. The Skill-based attack proposal `d20 + Weapon Skill ≥ 10 + Evasion − applicable AC penalties` remains under discussion, as does treatment of armor, shields, maneuvers and opposed noncombat checks.

**Open:** Movement Proficiency scaling, initiative roll/priority/tie procedure, whether base movement requires an independent action or is a standard turn allowance, SP budget and proficiency prices, handling/Skill conversion, physical speed lore/race features, initial/overlapping hostile zones and proficiency-versus-attribute terminology. No battle simulations or item conversions have run.

## November 22, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION: qualifying sleep restores BROKEN Shimmer Guard (HP PROTOTYPE ONLY)

**APPROVED OVERRIDE:** For the additive-HP replacement prototype, a Hero's Shimmer Guard that was shattered by emergency Break recovers at the **earlier of (a) completion of a qualifying sleep or (b) 24 hours since it broke**. A qualifying sleep therefore **ends the Break lockout early AND fully restores the Shimmer Reserve**, consistent with the already approved full-Shimmer-restoration-on-sleep rule. Sleep also restores ordinary HP by its separate approved Hero recovery rule (50% of maximum ordinary HP, or 100% if paired with medical attention). **No medical attention is required to reset Shimmer Guard.** The 51%-of-maximum-COMBINED-HP emergency threshold and complete negation of the triggering attack remain unchanged. The 24-hour timer remains an alternative recovery route when no qualifying sleep occurs; whether expiring that timer alone immediately refills the reserve without sleep remains UNRESOLVED. Exact minimum sleep duration and interruptions remain OPEN. This newer explicit correction **supersedes all earlier prototype text saying sleep cannot bypass the 24-hour Break lockout**. Existing operative wound-based combat is not replaced.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED ADDITIVE-HP PROTOTYPE: armor, carrying tradeoffs, and sleep healing (NOT YET OPERATIVE)

**Approval scope:** The author approved the latest proposed additive-HP armor-design test direction and the following restorative rules. These are approved **design decisions within the replacement prototype**; the old wound-based, quadratic live rules and their established item ratings remain operative until an authorized integrated conversion. The illustrative armor and equipment points below are **prototype test benchmarks**, not a universal conversion of existing materials, Quality, weapon catalogues, or individual Armor Rating/Weight statistics.

**Prototype foundation and intent:** Damage after armor is `max(0, eligible attack damage − effective covered armor)`, and armor is allowed to negate damage completely. Ordinary peer-versus-peer hits target roughly **three damaging landed attacks to incapacitation**, not three guaranteed attack attempts. Hero's approved Shimmer Reserve adds **100% of maximum ordinary HP**, with emergency Break triggered at **at least 51% of maximum COMBINED HP** in one post-armor hit while intact and dormant, negating that entire hit and disabling Shimmer for **24 hours**. Existing Weak Point targeting distinctions (actual exposed unarmored areas, protected gaps, and normal coverage) remain important; no invented universal chip-damage floor is approved. **Working HP candidate:** `max(1, 50 + 4×effective END)` is the current calibration baseline of the prototype; do not treat it as a completed globally migrated canon formula until all edge cases and scaling are ratified.

**Approved equipment-direction principle:** Combined **Weight capacity = effective STR + effective END**, so END benefits ordinary survival by contributing HP and by making better armor / gear combinations feasible. STR also contributes to Weight, offensive output and Technique as directed by the ongoing redesign. Equipment pieces should consume the **shared total equipped Weight budget**, rather than each receiving an independent permission check. Test ordinary armor with **no separate inherent SPD penalty** initially because Weight costs already compete with SPD allocations; preserve legitimate separate weapon-specific Hulking Speed caps and Precision weapon restrictions until the new rules are fully ratified. Actual encumbrance, negative-stat, eligibility, race, equipment-material and multiple-armor interactions require holistic conversion.

**Approved armor-ladder first prototype for testing (NOT finalized material/Quality catalogue):**

| Progression reference (not a level gate) | Light armor: protection / Weight | Medium: protection / Weight | Heavy: protection / Weight |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Level 10 | 10 / 6 | 20 / 12 | 35 / 22 |
| Level 50 | 40 / 20 | 75 / 35 | 110 / 55 |
| Level 100 | 65 / 35 | 115 / 55 | 165 / 85 |

These values were approved as **working calibration candidates**. They must be checked against legal weapon Weight, Handling, multi-slot coverage, economics, armor-penetration and attributes, before permanent item profiles or world inventory are rewritten. Illustrative example at level 100: an ordinary 240-point attack against armor 0/65/115/165 deals 240/175/125/75 HP damage respectively; a 370-HP target withstands 2/3/3/5 such landed hits.

**APPROVED NEW SLEEP-HEALING RULES for the HP prototype:** Recovery is a fraction of **maximum ORDINARY HP**, added to current ordinary HP and capped at that maximum, per qualifying sleep. Ordinary characters recover **10%** of maximum ordinary HP from sleep, or **20%** when sleep includes medical attention. Heroes recover **50%** of maximum ordinary HP from sleep, or **100%** with medical attention. **Hero Shimmer Reserve independently restores to full after qualifying sleep**, provided the Guard is available rather than broken/locked out. Sleep does **not** bypass the specifically approved **24-hour Shimmer Break lockout**; no ordinary-HP recovery itself repairs a broken Guard. Example Hero with ordinary max370 and ordinary HP100: sleep alone restores185 -> 285 HP, while sleep with medical attention restores up to full370. Ordinary character max370 regains37 after sleep, or74 with sleep and medical attention, subject to missing HP.

**Open for conversion:** A qualifying sleep's duration/interruptions; recovery rounding when percentages yield fractions; medical-attention skill, supplies and time; no-rest healing and supernatural healing; whether Shimmer Guard is replenished immediately at the end of the 24-hour Break timeout or only after a subsequent qualifying rest; precise reserve overflows, statuses, negative END and SPD, material and Weight equivalence, source-specific HP damage, non-Hero Legends, armor weak-point ratings, Healing Toxicity translation. Do not assume obsolete wound-based healing amounts automatically become new HP amounts. No simulator experiment was run for these rules.

## November 22, 2026 — AUTHOR-APPROVED Shimmer Guard rules for proposed additive-HP redesign (NOT YET OPERATIVE)

**Scope and precedence:** The author explicitly approved the following **Shimmer Guard design decisions for the experimental additive-HP combat replacement**. The existing wound-based combat and Shimmer Guard mechanics remain **operative canon** until the entire replacement is separately approved for implementation. This entry documents approved prototype decisions; it does **not** silently migrate existing campaigns, replace wound rules, or ratify an HP formula.

**100% Shimmer Reserve:** A qualifying Hero's Shimmer Reserve equals **100% of maximum ordinary HP**, so full combined maximum HP equals twice maximum ordinary HP. Ordinary damaging hits reduce ordinary HP; when it is depleted, Shimmer Guard activates and its reserve absorbs later damage. Damage from the ordinary hit that exhausts ordinary HP may carry through into the reserve under the proposed normal resolution, subject to later finalization of activation timing and injury/zero-HP edge cases. Shimmer Reserve is not an additional ordinary HP bonus to Endurance.

**Emergency Shimmer Break:** While Shimmer Guard is **intact and dormant**, evaluate a single incoming attack's **actual HP damage after armor and applicable mitigation**. If that damage is **at least 51% of the Hero's FULL COMBINED MAXIMUM HP (ordinary max HP + maximum Shimmer Reserve)**, Shimmer Guard **negates all HP damage from that attack**, immediately **breaks**, forfeits its entire unused Shimmer Reserve, and provides **no Shimmer protection for 24 hours**. Do not use 51% of ordinary HP, current HP, or remaining reserve. Evaluate the emergency threshold before deducting this attack's HP damage. A fully armored-blocked attack with zero actual HP damage cannot trigger it. For whole-number damage and HP, the trigger is `ceil(0.51 × combined maximum HP)`; e.g., ordinary HP 370, reserve 370, combined 740 -> trigger at **378** post-armor damage. On emergency break the Hero retains their preattack ordinary HP; other separately warranted non-HP effects are not automatically negated. Emergency break does not trigger after the reserve has already become active.

**Examples:** ordinary HP 90 / reserve 90 -> break at 92 damage; 210 / 210 -> at 215; 370 / 370 -> at 378. These ordinary HP values are **experimental reference builds** based on an **unapproved** proposed `max(1, 50 + 4×END)` formula; they do not approve that formula, armor ratings, damage calibration, healing or new equipment statistics.

**Still open:** precise normal HP/Shimmer overflow and activation timing; when/how an intact exhausted reserve returns; whether the 24-hour expiration also replenishes reserve or merely re-enables it; interactions with ongoing status damage, nonattack hazards, multiple hits from one offensive action, Shimmer healing/restoration, Legends, death versus incapacitation, and how HP-based Toxicity/healing replaces wound-based rules. No combat simulation has been run for this prototype.

---

## November 22, 2026 — LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL: all wound-based combat penalties removed

**COMPLETE REMOVAL:** Wounded, Gravely Wounded, and the additional Hero/Legend Shimmer Guard injury stages impose **no generic mechanical penalties**. Do not reduce STR, DEX, END, INT, SPD, derived Combat Speed, attack Power, Defense Power, hit/evasion difficulty, equipment eligibility, or any other combat calculation because a character has accumulated wounds. No percentage, flat, reduced, situational, pain-based, or simplified wound penalty replaces the removed rules. Pain and injury presentation may be narrated, but they do not impose mechanical penalties. **Incapacitated** remains the existing injury state that ordinarily prevents a character from acting.

**Progression retained:** Ordinary characters retain their three injury advancements: Wounded → Gravely Wounded → Incapacitated. Heroes and Legends retain their two additional Shimmer Guard injury stages: Wounded → Guard I → Guard II → Gravely Wounded → Incapacitated. Guard I and Guard II are additional injury capacity, not low-penalty stages. Existing injury thresholds, advancement from resolved attacks, healing, prognosis, and Hero/Legend distinctions remain unchanged.

**Equipment and initiative:** Calculate equipment handling shortfalls from actual current effective attributes and applicable non-wound modifiers. An injury state cannot reduce an attribute, create a new equipment shortfall, or make an otherwise qualified item ineligible. Existing shortfall penalties remain applicable when a genuine independent stat shortfall exists. Combat Speed and initiative likewise receive no wound-derived stat reduction.

**Supersedes:** The former Wounded −5%, Gravely Wounded −10%, and Incapacitated −15% effective-attribute penalties; the Hero/Legend “still −5%” Guard stages; and every cross-reference that recalculated equipment eligibility or SPD after a wound. Independent hostile statuses and their own explicitly approved effects are unaffected.

**Nonquadratic redesign boundary:** The proposed Attack Grade / Defense Grade conversion and rounded grade-to-injury thresholds remain **PROVISIONAL TEST MATERIAL, NOT CANON**. The existing quadratic Power/Defense formulas and impact thresholds remain operative until separately approved.

**Simulation compatibility:** The connected Combat Lab 0.7.0 warrior engine still applies its old wound factor and does not implement Attack/Defense Grades. Its full-duel outputs do not yet represent this approval; a separate simulator update is required before those duels can test the revised rules.
