## November 22, 2026 — APPROVED UNIVERSAL: all active status-inflicting actions are ATTACKS

**Latest explicit author ruling:** **Every combat action that actually attempts to inflict a hostile status effect is an OFFENSIVE ATTACK**, whether or not that action deals ordinary direct damage or physical wounds. This includes Jester **Binding**, non-damaging magical status applications and any other eligible offensive maneuver whose result is a hostile status condition. Such actions **count toward the existing maximum of ONE offensive attack action per combatant's turn** and must obey their actual declared action count (one-action or two-action form as otherwise approved), accuracy/contest, relevant resistance, legal source/reach, and true mana/Channel Limit cost. Not doing damage never qualifies a status-inflicting action as harmless movement, preparation or another nonoffensive free attack. A failed qualifying attempt **still consumes that committed offensive attack action**. No generic exemption for pure status magic, no automatic new attack for maintaining already ongoing statuses, and no change to ordinary nonoffensive cleansing/assistance such as removing an ally's status. Do not conflate passive condition exposure without a fresh combat action with a new action cost.

**Approved Jestercraft application:** Binding is a **non-damaging status attack**. Its active **Binding Power equals the Jester's legal ordinary magical Attack Power for the SAME invested base offensive MP**, `P_Binding(MP)=P_magic_attack(MP)`; it uses the existing ordinary magical Power scaling rather than separate invented discount/multiplier. Bindings grow as additional new **mana-woven thread extensions from a lawful Draped environmental tile or the Jester's qualifying Worn Draped source** within 5 feet of the intended target. Their originating tile/Stitch **is not used up or downgraded**; a rug may be reused for later separately paid attacks. An unrestricted number of captives may be bound **if each has separately been lawfully attacked**; captives attempt eligible escape individually and a successful one is freed of their own restraint. Escaping does not destroy the reusable environmental source: physically clearing it requires directly removing/destroying the tile itself, under valid material/attack rules. Binding from an old rug creates no free bonus attack, no permanent automatic re-capture and no mana refund. Removing the source's effect on already emitted bindings remains to be specified.

**Preserve preexisting exceptions:** Restraint severity already defined by the relevant Bound rules still governs whether a target can legally attempt self-escape (including previously approved helpless thresholds), and specific subclass/ability permissions remain controlling. The above universal attack-classification change does not authorize two attacks per turn simply because one does no damage.

---

## November 22, 2026 — LATEST APPROVED UNIVERSAL TECHNIQUE FORMULA: highest STR or DEX

**AUTHOR OVERRIDE / OPERATIVE RULE:** 

`Technique = max(effective STR, effective DEX)`.

Use the **higher** of the acting character's actual effective **STR** and **DEX**, after applicable approved species and other modifiers, without summing the two attributes or doubling STR. This **explicitly supersedes** the prior `Technique = 2×effective STR + effective DEX` and the earlier grapple-control `G=2×STR+DEX` formula **everywhere those older values were previously operative**, including grapple initiation/escape/holding, Feint, trips, disarms, shoves, throws, contested departure/entry through weapon zones, and Jestercraft Unravel contests. Existing particular technique eligibility/action rules and the **universal opposed ratio → Naked-d20 six-category** check method stay in place; do not interpret the new formula as free extra actions, damage or a new attack roll. Both Strength-focused and Dexterity-focused specialists can now be equally capable at Technique, while pure INT investment does not raise it. **Earlier entries below describing STR as double-weighted remain in the historical changelog, expressly superseded by this section**. Zero and otherwise undefined opposed-score cases follow the universal opposed-check framework, not invented new defaults.

**Unravel tie-in:** A newly introduced thread pathway into real enemy weapon-control territory is resisted using the **single highest valid defending character's current Technique** under existing strongest-controller movement principles; zones still require their actual physical reach, readiness and geometry. The one-easier-category **Threadwalk movement** modifier remains specific to the Jester moving/jumping through a zone, not to remotely invading fabric. An ordinary legal Jester with **one usable Worn Regalia Stitch now qualifies as a mobile Draped Binding origin** within five feet; separate new author approval allows a whole source Stitch to create **four qualifying 5-foot Draped environmental tiles** under environmental throughput `max(1,ceil(full Jestercraft CL/5))`. These are fabric-sourcing rules and do not modify ordinary reach of weapons, attack Power or damage.

## November 22, 2026 — Jestercraft Unravel challenges the strongest zone controller (AUTHOR APPROVED)

A Jester adding new environmental thread/fabric through **Unravel** into tiles covered by hostile **real zones of control** must succeed in **one universal opposed Technique check versus the SINGLE STRONGEST actually eligible defending controller**, as in the already approved movement-zone contest; **not one check per creature, thread, or tile**. Existing `Technique=2×effective STR+effective DEX`, ratio-to-Naked-d20 table, physical weapon reach/geometry, and legitimate Pressure apply. An eligible warrior can maintain the backline squares they actually control against advancing threads. A distant uncontrolled tile is not covered simply because a high-Technique fighter is elsewhere in the room. Success lets the material enter otherwise legally accessible controlled terrain; failure blocks that contested incursion on that action, not every separate physically accessible unopposed destination. Unravel's novel binding attack still requires a qualifying Draped-origin source within **5 feet of the target**; no automatically generated binding/injury merely from establishing fabric. The unrelated approved **Threadwalk movement** one-easier-category Technique benefit is not a free bonus for controlling fabric remotely. Owner: `05_Jestercraft.md`.

## November 22, 2026 — Threadwalk: easier Technique checks through control zones (JESTERCRAFT AUTHOR APPROVED)

When a Jester genuinely **Threadwalks or Threadsprings** along/from legally supporting Threaded-or-denser fabric, any **Technique check required to pass through an enemy's zone of control** is **one Naked-d20 difficulty category easier**. Apply after establishing the otherwise lawful Technique contest and other applicable modifiers, using the existing six-category table and normal clamping. This is not a numeric −1 to a d20 roll, does not stack per rope/tile, does not bypass actual physical/reach control or impossible geometry, and does not enhance ordinary attack, Feint, grapple, or unrelated Technique checks. The Skill's owning reference is `05_Jestercraft.md`. The author separately directs **one tightrope deployment per Unravel action**; precise fabric expansion, cost and environmental medium density remain unapproved proposals.

## November 22, 2026 — Feint: one-action Technique counter to prepared defense (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**New universal combat action: Feint.** A combatant may spend **one of their two actions** to present a credible **false attack** intended to lure an opponent into committing an already prepared defensive response. A Feint requires **winning a Technique check** against the defender, using the existing **Technique = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX** characteristic and otherwise applicable contested-check system. It is **not a real damaging attack**. The attacker must have a plausible physical/targeting opportunity to present the false threat and must aim the bluff at an actual or reasonably believed prepared defense; successful deception triggers an eligible prepared defensive response matching the announced apparent attack line, target, and trigger. If the check fails, the opponent is not compelled to expend that prepared response. The Feint itself does not deal Attack Power, injuries, elemental status, Pressure Points, or other automatic damage.

**What is baited:** A successfully deceived defender **commits/expends the prepared defensive response**, even though the threatened attack is false. The guard is triggered at the announced apparent line; if the defense is physical material or creates a lasting shield/barrier, its **physical material and remaining durability do not vanish or take damage just because the Feint succeeded**. The defense may still occupy the location to which it was moved, and any separate maintained/ongoing effects obey their own durations. The Feint may therefore misposition a Jester's prepared Regalia or waste a prepared Parry/magical reaction, but does **not** dismantle a permanently maintained obstacle, erase ordinary shield Guard, negate real armor, permit teleporting, bypass geometry, or automatically grant a hit. A Parry misled by a Feint earns **no Parry Pressure Points**, because it did not prevent a real would-be body hit.

**Action-economy open edge:** The author approved the **one-action Feint and mandatory successful Technique check**, not yet a separate classification of Feint against the turn's **one offensive-attack-action maximum**. **PROPOSAL, NOT YET APPROVED:** treat a Feint as a nondamaging setup maneuver that does not itself consume the offensive-attack allowance, permitting an otherwise eligible one-action Quick Attack as the second action, subject to reach, attack difficulty and all other rules. Whether a failed Feint is detectable, whether a defender can choose to ignore a successful bluff, the exact faked-line eligibility in unusual attacks, and any special feint-vs-feint reactions remain OPEN. For an ordinary prepared reactive defense, retain its already established action reservation, legal declared trigger and expiration; Feint does not create a free defensive response that was not prepared.

---

## November 21, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Flat magical wound-healing Toxicity (LATEST)

**CURRENT CANON:** Each wound actually healed by magic adds **20 percentage points of Toxicity** to the recipient, regardless of ordinary wound severity or Shimmer Guard stage. For multiple wounds healed, add 20 percentage points per wound; do not charge for wounds left unhealed. This **supersedes** the historical superficial 5%, first ordinary injury 20%, first Shimmer Guard injury 10%, second Guard injury 15%, and other wound-tier-dependent healing charges. Existing cumulative Toxicity thresholds and mana-potion restoration formula remain unchanged. Healing-potion potency, rest-based Toxicity removal and other open implementation issues are not resolved here.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Physical damage forms do not modify injury after Impact (LATEST)

**Approved operative rule (supersedes the unresolved November 8 form-injury boundary):** For ordinary lawful physical attacks, calculate final delivered **Power** and the target's applicable **Defense** using the attack's actual weapon mode, physical damage form, armor penetration, coverage, material, and other approved modifiers. Once the attack makes lawful contact and resolves any interception or separate defensive effects, compute **Impact = Power / Defense** and apply the existing strict Impact-to-injury stages **directly**. **Slashing, Blunt and Piercing do not apply any further injury-stage multiplier, adjustment, cap, or post-Impact conversion. A wound is a wound.**

Preserve their existing distinctions **before** Impact: Slashing 0%, Blunt 25%, Piercing 50% ordinary covered-armor penetration, as well as each weapon mode's approved Power rating, legal reach, targeting and coverage. The historical Slashing 5/5, Blunt 3/5 and Piercing 1/5 *relative injury identities* are retired as mechanical guidance; they are **not multipliers**, and no form-specific wound-severity transformation is pending. This approval does not bypass armor, shield contact, hit chance, defenses, status, special abilities, or other existing restrictions. Existing Impact boundaries remain: **Impact <= 0.75 = superficial/0 stages; > 0.75 through 1.75 = Solid/1; > 1.75 through 3 = Devastating/2; > 3 = Overwhelming/3**, subject to separately approved wound/Guard handling. Other unresolved encounter mechanics are not decided by this ruling.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Structural Defense ladder and spell/ritual offensive benchmarks (LATEST)

**Current approved structural Defense Power reference values:** ordinary wood/doors **1,000**; thick timber/thin stone **8,000**; castle masonry **12,500**; reinforced stone/substantial metal **40,000**; extreme ordinary nonmagical fortifications **80,000**. The four non-castle values previously designated experimental are **now approved**. For eligible non-fragile objects, use the existing wound-system concept and its applicable thresholds rather than a generic independent building HP pool; only areas lawfully covered and damageable can receive the attack's wound, including a whole castle when the whole structure genuinely qualifies. Numerical classification of a successful threshold crossing is not automatic through-thickness penetration or unsupported collapse. Physical martial attacks can apply against the same structural defenses when otherwise eligible; defense typing, structural geometry, coverage, breach/collapse mapping and specific object wear rules remain to finalize.

**Offensive casting reference now approved:** `(governing attribute² + eligible IR²) × M_MP` at neutral tuning, M for base MP 0/1/2/4/8/12/20 = **0.55/1.00/1.35/1.70/2.05/2.25/2.51**, maximum **20 MP** in the base Power component before separate legal area/duration costs. A **10-minute ritual channels at most 4× applicable full CL**, with actual mana supply and legal spell-cost constraints unchanged. Mastery-level assumptions from prior illustrative six-level Kharak comparison are *not* automatically approved. This supersedes prior descriptions of these numerical benchmarks as unapproved; no structural simulator run is claimed.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Castle Defense 12,500 and structural breakthrough benchmarks (LATEST)

**APPROVED:** The ordinary unenchanted castle-masonry **structural Defense Power baseline is 12,500** for the current wound-based structural calibration (instead of the earlier proposed 17,000 or 13,225). This is a structure-defense reference benchmark, **not** an automatic Boughsteel armor AR/MR rating or a universal rating for every castle architecture or enchanted fortification. Apply existing wound/impact ratios provisionally when benchmarking structures; actual affected scope still follows the previously approved rule that only the area the attack can cover and damage is wounded.

**Author-selected progression targets:** A reasonably specialized **level-1** offensive caster should be able to penetrate ordinary wood; about **level 25** should penetrate thick wood or thin stone; about **level 50** should consistently inflict meaningful wound damage to ordinary castle masonry without having to rely on a ritual; about **level 75** should penetrate substantial/reinforced stone or metal; by **level 100**, a suitably developed caster should penetrate the upper end of ordinary nonmagical material/fortification defenses. This last design goal does not override specifically resistant divine, unique, or enchanted materials. 'Penetrate' in these numerical models means surpassing the first damaging wound threshold **when the attack successfully lands on a legally covered part**; a full through-hole still depends on thickness, scope and structural wound description. Ritually invested attacks can sometimes cross a tier earlier, preserving the previous level-25 small-castle-breach ritual objective.

**Calculated candidate structural-Defense tiers, NOT YET CANON except 12,500 castle:** Ordinary wood/doors **1,000**; thick timber/thin stone **8,000**; castle masonry **12,500 (APPROVED)**; reinforced stone/structural metal **40,000**; extreme nonmagical structures **80,000**. The other four are explicitly **PROPOSALS** pending further confirmation; they are not universal material durability values. These bands were fit to an experimental reference Pyromancer build and must remain independently adjustable for thickness/material, attack type, and magical protection.

**Manual calculation fixture, NOT new canon:** Kharak starting STR22/DEX15/END20/INT17/SPD14, racial +25% INT, all earned allocated points divided evenly into INT and END, every-five-level stat increments, neutral Quality I/I/II/III/IV/V implements with approved IR26/26/30/35/40/48, and working (NOT approved as a final spell law) `P=(effective INT²+IR²) × proposed MP multiplier`. Levels 1/10/25/50/75/100 one-action legal-cost tier investments 1/2/4/8/12/12 MP yield approximate Power **1,128 / 2,566 / 7,151 / 21,013 / 43,102 / 70,209**. Ten-minute ritual experimental capacity and Mastery fixture, capped at 20 MP base offensive investment, yield **2,312 / 4,772 / 10,558 / 25,728 / 48,082 / 78,322** Power. One-action spells respectively first exceed the first wound threshold of proposed Defense tiers 1,000 at level1; 8,000 at level25; approved 12,500 at level50; proposed 40,000 at level75; proposed 80,000 at level100. At level25 a 10-minute ritual can already cause a Solid wound on 12,500-Defense masonry, whereas an ordinary one-action spell cannot; 'about level50 consistently damages castle' refers to ordinary casting against this reference, not immunity to lower-level rituals.

**Critical boundaries:** MP-to-Power multiplier chart (0 MP×0.55, 1×1, 2×1.35, 4×1.70, 8×2.05, 12×2.25, 20×2.51) remains the author's agreed **experimental starting chart, not final canon**. Ten-minute ritual throughput multiplier ×4, illustrative Mastery rank values 0/3/6/9/12/15, gear affordability, and exact area/duration modifier prices remain **UNAPPROVED fixtures**. Do not infer attack success probability, full breaching of thick walls, automatic castle-wide area, normal spell eligibility of every fixture, or Combat Lab simulations. No simulation was run for these structural outputs.

## November 20, 2026 — APPROVED ELEMENTAL STATUS RESISTANCE FORMULA (LATEST)

**Author-approved formula for each applicable elemental status type:**

```text
Elemental SR = ceil( ((effective END + 0.65 × applicable MR)^2 × ER_element) / 0.70 )
```

Here **effective END** includes qualifying equipment bonuses and normal effective-attribute effects; **applicable MR** means the legal Magic Resistance for the relevant target/status context, subject to existing armor coverage, named materials, exceptions and separate defenses; **ER_element** is the *applicable existing named element-specific resistance multiplier* (use **1** where none applies). Do not invent a new ER source or assume a numerical ER value for an unresolved material. **Ceiling is applied once at the end** of the calculation, not separately to MR weighting, the squared term, or the elemental multiplier. MR coefficient **0.65** and divisor **0.70** are now explicitly **CANON**, superseding older 'unapproved typed SR equation' notices. This replaces the older standalone **SR = effective END²** calculation for *elemental typed statuses*; retain that earlier value only where a distinct rule specifically calls for END² outside this elemental-SR procedure.

**Normal legal elemental contact contributes 100% of its eligible delivered elemental Power to the matching status buildup**, without the old universal ×0.70 offense coefficient. Maintain the existing actual-contact, interception, medium, power, magnitude, three-turn same-element accumulation/refresh, and element-specific resistance rules. **Do not equate buildup to final injury stages or physical weapon damage**: use the actual eligible *elemental* Power reaching the status-delivery step. Direct-injury Power/damage and other spell damage calculations are not automatically multiplied by 1/0.70 or changed by this status-only normalization. Do not remove independent status application requirements, defenses, or specific elemental effects.

**Preserve attack-form exceptions relative to the new 100% ordinary buildup:** Ordinary Fire **100%**, Blue Flame **50%**, White Flame **0% and no refresh**; Wakecraft's dedicated no-damage Freeze Attack **200%** of otherwise equivalent eligible Freeze magnitude; other established spell, enchantment or status-specific adjustments apply only when already canonically defined. Applicable thresholds remain **0.5×, 1×, and 4× elemental SR** with element-specific exceptions (including Ice's separately defined 12× Entombed threshold); this update does not change severity outcomes, tick cadence, durations or any previously approved attack costs.

**Rationale and balance boundary:** Algebraically relocates the experimental 70% ordinary elemental-buildup coefficient into the precomputed defensive threshold: `Power×0.70 / OldSR = Power / (OldSR/0.70)`, apart from final rounding. E.g., effective END **50**, MR **40**, ER **1** gives `ceil((50+0.65×40)^2/0.70) = 8,252`, and a lawful elemental Power **3,000** adds **3,000** ordinary buildup. The current combat simulations were performed under their recorded historical experimental policies; **no post-approval balance rerun or simulator engine migration is claimed**. This is not a blanket approval of an independent formula for Psycraft/mental or other non-elemental status systems; their previously approved procedures remain. Interaction of Water's percentage MR erosion with recalculating ongoing typed SR and other previously open timing/rounding interactions remains **UNRESOLVED**.

**Precedence:** Any older sections here or in `00_Mystaria_Canon_Index.md`, `10_Canon_Changelog.md`, or `22_Combat_Endurance_and_Shimmer_Guard.md` saying the 0.65 MR weighting, typed elemental SR combination, or universal 0.70 elemental coefficient are 'unapproved' or that elemental SR uses END² alone are **SUPERSEDED** by this entry. Other historic approvals (elemental weapon Power I–VI, three-flame penetration, Wakecraft forms and status effects) stand.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Finisher interruption, Charm refresh, modular status duration (LATEST)

**FINISHER INTERRUPTIONS (APPROVED):** An announced physical Finisher still costs **two actions**, must be performed from **within 5 feet**, has **×4 damage rather than automatic death**, and resolves **at the start of the attacker's next turn**. Before resolution, the Finisher is interrupted if **(a)** the intended victim **ceases to be an eligible target** (for instance, awakening from Sleep, ending qualifying immobilization, or losing the Severity II / 4× Charm that supplied eligibility), or **(b)** the attacker **can no longer complete the attack** (for example, target moved out of required range or attacker disarmed). A mere hit on the preparing attacker does **not** automatically interrupt absent a real loss of qualification or attack feasibility. No new guaranteed-hit or defense-bypass mechanic is conferred. Other specific feasibility disputes follow ordinary action/positioning rules, not invented auto-interrupt triggers.

**CHARM DURATION (APPROVED):** Both **Severity I / 1× Charm** (subtle friendliness/suggestibility and charmer-specific combat penalties) and **Severity II / 4× Charm** (genuine allegiance change and competent hostile fighting) have a **base duration of three rounds**. **Applying additional Charm status refreshes the active Charm duration**. Precise timing if refreshing with a lower-severity application, and whether ongoing accumulation versus actual applied status counts as refresh, are left to general status application rules where specified; do not silently promote a 1× Charm to 4× without the required status threshold. Existing breaking conditions remain: attacks by the charmer or the charmer's allies break Charm **after the attack resolves**, at either severity. Severity I Charm remains **ineligible** for physical Finishers; Severity II qualifies.

**MODULAR STATUS DURATION (APPROVED):** **All statuses** can have their duration changed through the universal **modular spell Duration option**, **provided that the final triggering spell** has legally paid the extra duration price. Earlier buildup hits do not pay that price on behalf of the triggering spell, and extra buildup by itself does not constitute free extended duration. Status severity thresholds (1× / 4× for the mental-control examples), required magical Power, effect eligibility, normal defenses, Channel Limit, legal casting, duration-modifier price status, and specific exceptions remain unchanged unless explicitly revised. Exact numerical duration modifiers/cost ladder remain as previously marked (some provisional), not newly approved here.

**PRECEDENCE:** This entry resolves prior open Charm base duration and Finisher interruption criteria in the November 20 Psychic/status entries; supersedes the words describing Charm duration or Finisher interruption as wholly open. No combat simulator results are claimed.

---

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR CORRECTION: Mental status thresholds 1×/4×; Finisher requires strong Charm (LATEST)

**Approved correction, controlling over the November 20 mental-status entries below:** The five mental conditions (Confusion, Anger, Fear, Charm, Sleep) use **exactly two severities**, triggered at **1× buildup (Severity I)** and **4× buildup (Severity II)**, consistent with other statuses. The previous entries incorrectly describing **2× as Severity II**, or suggesting that **4× is merely an optional duration-overbuild benchmark**, are **SUPERSEDED**. No extra intermediate severity is created. The attacker may release buildup on the same attack that applies it, or continue buildup toward 4×. Severity I/II effects and durations are those explicitly specified in the preceding approved status record: Confusion/Anger/Fear **1 round at 1×; 3 rounds at 4×**; Sleep **up to 3 rounds at 1×; 1 minute at 4×**; Charm **suggestibility and source-only attack penalty at 1×; active allegiance switch at 4×**. Charm and Sleep still halve applicable status-application magical Power. Extra spell duration investment and exact Charm durations remain unresolved.

**Finisher eligibility clarification:** **Severity I (1×) Charm does NOT make a creature eligible for a Finisher.** **Severity II (4×) Charm DOES.** Sleep and qualifying immobilization remain Finisher-eligible as previously approved. No additional automatic helplessness, instant death, or new Finisher defense bypass is implied. Older statements simply saying 'Charm makes eligible' are qualified by this ruling. All other previously approved Finisher rules are unchanged: two actions, attacker within 5 feet, ×4 damage, resolves at the start of the attacker's next turn and can be interrupted.

**Correction to the immediately preceding emotional-status entry:** The author's existing shared status system has **two severity thresholds: 1× buildup for Severity I and 2× buildup for Severity II**. However, the author's individual examples explicitly compare **1× release to 4× accumulated buildup**, including Confusion/Anger/Fear durations of **1 round at 1× and 3 rounds at 4×**, and Sleep of **up to 3 rounds at 1× and up to 1 minute at 4×**. **Do not reinterpret 4× as 2× or assert that releasing at 2× automatically gives the full 4× duration.** The details for duration when releasing between these named benchmarks, and how greater buildup affects the two severity profiles, remain OPEN. ## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR DECISION: Psychic emotional control, subtle Cipherlight, and physical Finishers (LATEST)

**Status:** Author-selected mechanics. Earlier exploratory four-stage (1× to 4×) emotional-control duration interpolation is superseded by the existing **two-severity thresholds at 1× and 2× status buildup**. Emotional effects accumulate via attacks; the attacker chooses whether to continue buildup or release it, including on the same attack that increases it. An overwhelmingly powerful attacker can therefore activate a weak victim's condition immediately, subject to normal resistance. The exact decay of unreleased buildup, interaction with other conditions, and extra MP to extend duration remain OPEN.

**Confusion:** Severity I lasts **1 round**, Severity II **3 rounds**; severity changes duration, not the D3 procedure. When the affected creature attacks, casts a spell, or makes an analogous qualifying action, roll **D3**: **1** = Psychic chooses a valid target other than the creature's intended target, potentially exploiting the confusion; if no valid alternative exists, action fails harmlessly or fires in a useless direction. **2** = action wasted. **3** = action resolves as intended. This also affects beneficial spells/actions when qualifying; exact boundaries for non-targeted or area effects are OPEN.

**Anger:** Severity I **1 round**, Severity II **3 rounds**. Affected creature attacks the nearest creature other than itself; eligible offensive spells may be used when it can take an action. Ties, inability to attack, and action selection remain OPEN.

**Fear:** Severity I **1 round**, Severity II **3 rounds**. Affected creature retreats from the source as quickly as possible, using its actions to dash or magical movement when possible; if cornered, cowers without acting. Appropriate movement spells are permitted. Exact path selection, source disappearance and what counts as cornered remain OPEN.

**Charm (advanced):** Status application uses **half the attacker's normal applicable magical Power** (not half the result after successful application). At Severity I, the victim is suggestible, generally regards the charmer as a friend outside combat, and in combat suffers **−2 accuracy and −20% attack Power only when attacking the charmer**, not the charmer's allies. This lesser charm is subtle: a typical victim does not believe they were charmed. At Severity II, the victim **switches sides** and fights sincerely and competently for the charmer, without deliberate self-sabotage or suicidal action; it is not permission to deliberately remove oneself from battle through ineffective choices. Victims remember their actions afterward. **Either severity breaks if the charmer OR one of the charmer's allies attacks the victim, only AFTER the provoking attack has resolved**. Precise Charm duration, control hierarchy, and noncombat behavioral boundaries remain OPEN.

**Sleep (advanced):** Status application uses **half normal applicable magical Power**. Severity I puts victim to sleep for **up to 3 rounds**, Severity II for **up to 1 minute**. A creature wakes if shaken or struck; an ally can spend **one action** to wake them, or use a qualifying ranged attack or spell to wake them. Sleep itself is not automatic death, and normal attack/Finisher/Execute legality remains relevant. Trivial versus damaging spell contact, environment and timing of awakening remain OPEN.

**Subtle Psycraft Cipherlight:** All Psychics must have a way to deliver appropriate hypnosis/mental influences **unobtrusively outside combat**, by **taking longer rather than spending more mana**. It does not waive a suitable mandatory focusing gem or existing Cipherlight delivery, resistance and contest restrictions. Exact additional time and detection procedure remain OPEN.

**Physical Finishers:** Separate from magical instant-kill **Executes**. A Finisher takes **two actions**, is available to physical and magical characters, requires the attacker to be **within 5 feet of the target**, and deals **×4 damage** when resolved, **not automatic instant death**. It resolves **at the start of the attacker's NEXT turn**, creating a window for interruption. Creatures affected by **Charm, Sleep, or immobilization** are eligible targets. Whether either Charm severity alone suffices, which immobilizations count, what preserves or interrupts a declared Finisher, exact attack/damage timing, armor/defense interaction, and whether eligibility is rechecked at resolution remain OPEN. Do not silently add an automatic success or bypass ordinary defenses.

**Priority integration:** Psycraft, universal status buildup, magical contests, and combat action timing should reference these rulings; exact numeric status Power formula is still in calibration. This is not an approval of a combat-simulator implementation or test result.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: Mana potion catalogue, Toxicity implementation, extra refill doses and obsolete shop warning (LATEST)

**Approved offensive-price correction:** the provisional base offensive spell investment ladder now reads **0 MP cantrip; 1 MP basic; 2 MP enhanced; 4 MP strong; 8 MP greater; 12 MP superior; 20 MP maximum**, before area, duration or additional modifiers. The author-selected price benchmarks are retained as the **current WIP ladder**, not a verified final spell-Power-per-MP formula; the previous 11 MP superior and 18 MP maximum proposals are superseded.

**Approved Toxicity treatment:** rename Heal Burn to **Toxicity** throughout current usage. Toxicity can accrue from healing, mana restoration and other explicitly eligible effects. Using **maximum usable MP after Soul Strain** as the denominator, drinking a mana potion grants Toxicity equal to **100% × actual MP restored / current usable maximum MP**, with excess potion restoration wasted once MP is full. Toxicity is cumulative. At **100%** a recipient has a total of **1 Toxicity wound**, at **150%** total **2 wounds**, at **200%** total **3**, then one more total wound every additional 50 percentage points. Wounds are **threshold-based totals**, not added again each time a potion is consumed while already above a threshold; effects and wounds caused by reaching thresholds cannot be healed until rest permits it. Exact rest-based Toxicity reduction and release from wound-healing lock are still OPEN. The historical per-injury healing-Toxicity benchmarks are SUPERSEDED by the November 21, 2026 flat +20 percentage points per magically healed wound rule.

**Approved tiered mana-restoration potion proposal, now baseline for continued calibration:** One ordinary drink restores **Tier I (level ~10): 5 MP at 6 Gems; II (~25): 10 MP at 12 Gems; III (~50): 20 MP at 24 Gems; IV (~75): 40 MP at 48 Gems; V (~100): 80 MP at 96 Gems**. Effective potion restoration is capped at usable missing MP. The comparable **one-dose-per-day refillable** prices are **60 / 120 / 240 / 480 / 960 Gems**, respectively. At a given tier, each extra daily drink/charge on a refillable adds **50% of the tier's base refillable price**, repeatedly, with no numeric maximum count yet selected. Thus an item with N daily doses costs **base × [1 + 0.5 × (N−1)]**, before any as-yet-unapproved modifiers; each dose is a separate potion drinking/use opportunity. Replenishes **one use per daily charge**; additional purchased charges each also refill once per day. Refillable potion terminology, contents/vessel and interactions with rest or day boundaries remain to detail. The approximate 1:10 disposable-to-refillable price relationship and current economy references remain. Do not assume potions are manufactured or ubiquitous without crafting/availability rules.

**Approved use and threat:** Drawing a potion costs **one action**; drinking costs **one action**; drawing during a grapple needs a **Technique check** (numerical check and outcomes TBD). Eligible enemy prepared attacks or grapples can interrupt access or destroy the potion via ordinary legal interaction; no free reaction or extra attacks. Mana potions restore actual MP without changing maximum MP. Toxicity is shared with healing and future expressly eligible buffs, making replenishment trade off against injury treatment.

**Critical source-quality warning:** The old **'Consumables — Early Stock' Gem Shop inventory** in `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` (including old **20-Gem Mana Dew** and healing vial offers) is **OUTDATED / NOT A CURRENT PRICING REFERENCE**. It must be comprehensively revised before used for offers, economic comparisons, or future consumable balancing. Preserve historical records but never treat that catalogue as authoritative current market pricing. The present tiered potion reference and latest 3,200-Gem level-100 equipment portfolio, 30%-of-lifetime-earnings consumable allocation take priority.

**OPEN:** Healing-potion potency/toxicity pricing integration; short-rest / long-rest Toxicity clearing and treatment of threshold wounds; mana potion sourcing, alchemical craft requirements, inventory and cost materials; potion usability by zero-natural-MP Warlocks; crafting of permanent daily-refill vessels; refill trigger timing and stacking; precise MP-to-Power conversion; updated skill-specific spell costs; marginal price/value effect of MP cost-modifying implements.

## November 20, 2026 — AUTHOR APPROVAL: 20-MP offensive benchmark and Toxicity resource-potion design (LATEST)

**Offensive spell investment, author-selected WIP design:** revise earlier **Greater** benchmark to **8 MP**, retain **Superior 11 MP**, and set **maximum unmodified base-effect/Power investment at 20 MP**, before duration, area, and other spell modifiers. Retain previously discussed basic 1, enhanced 2, strong 4, and 0-MP cantrip as **working ladder**, not a fully calibrated/approved MP-to-Power equation. Actual impact of additional mana on Spell Power and any overall price multiplier remain under testing.

**Rename:** The former **Heal Burn** mechanic is now **Toxicity**. Toxicity is a shared drawback accrued from relevant restorative effects, including healing and new mana-restoration potions, and potentially later potion buffs/other specified effects. This is a new balance subsystem rather than a blanket grant of potions/crafting ranks.

**Author-directed Toxicity thresholds:** accumulated Toxicity reaches harmful wound territory at approximately **100%**, with **1 wound at 100%, 2 wounds at 150% and +1 wound at each additional 50 percentage points** (200%→3, 250%→4, etc.). These Toxicity wounds **cannot be healed until the recipient rests**. The speaker also said 'once you exceed 100%'; the exact boundary wording is ambiguous and requires confirmation; **100% inclusive** is the working reading of the explicit 'at 100%, it's 1 wound' example. The historical Heal Burn per-injury percentage schedule (superficial 5%, first ordinary injury 20%, Guard 10%/15%, etc.) is SUPERSEDED by the November 21, 2026 flat +20 percentage points per magically healed wound rule. Short rest/long rest Toxicity reduction amounts and how temporary wound locks lift remain to be integrated; do not invent cures or permanent recovery impairment.

**Soul Strain interaction:** for percentage-based Toxicity calculations, treat **maximum usable MP after Soul Strain** as the character's maximum mana, not the hypothetical unstrained natural reserve. Numerical mana restoration/Toxicity conversion is not yet approved. Avoid assuming Toxicity automatically lowers maximum MP, or that existing Soul Strain was removed.

**Mana potions and action economy:** Mana-restoring consumables are intended to restore actual spendable MP (up to the user's usable maximum). The user approves a tactical sequence requiring **one action to draw a potion and one action to drink it**; enemies can exploit legal prepared attacks on the potion or interfere with the user. **Drawing an item during a grapple requires a Technique check**, with its exact difficulty and opposed-resolution mechanics pending. This rule does not create additional attacks or waive reaction legality.

**Pricing direction:** propose tier-appropriate single-use mana potions priced at **roughly one-tenth of a full comparable item** of the tier, and **once-per-day replenishing/refillable potions** costing as much as the full comparable item. Use the **current** approved tenfold-redominated economic framework: level 10/25/50/75/100 primary item reference costs **60/120/240/480/960 Gems**, endgame wealth **3,200 Gems**, and consumables **30% of lifetime gross earnings (~2,014 Gems through level 100)**. Specific mana restoration values, tier potion prices, Toxicity-per-restored-MP equation, refillable daily charge count, exact potion availability and crafting are **PROPOSALS pending approval**. The older 20-Gem 'Mana Dew' shop offer requires reconciliation, not silent deletion.

**Other unresolved design issues:** immunity to healing above the Toxicity limit, how lasting/rest-locked wounds interact with preexisting injury states, blood/liver toxicity remedies, Nymbrin potion manufacturing and recipes, action interruption, whether unused healing/potion strength creates Toxicity, and handling of Warlocks' zero natural MP/external reserves.

## November 19, 2026 — Initiative based on Combat Speed (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Supersedes the earlier initiative-on-effective-SPD rule.** Determine combatant initiative using **current ordinary Combat Speed = effective SPD + effective DEX/4**, incorporating the approved worn-armor SPD percentage applied to the combined statistic and applicable wound/equipment-shortfall penalties. Higher Combat Speed goes first. **Exact Combat Speed ties** retain existing priorities: Heroes/Legends before ordinary combatants; player characters before tied NPC Heroes/Legends; the DM decides remaining ties and the relative order stays fixed. Movement remains based on actual SPD; this change does not grant extra movement or attacks. Previously approved temporary Dash Combat Speed bonuses do not retroactively change already-established initiative order. Exact initiative refresh timing after real mid-combat statistic changes remains open. Prior statements in this document limiting Combat Speed exclusively to hit/evasion, or requiring effective SPD for initiative, are **historical and superseded on initiative only**; other procedures remain unchanged.

---

## November 19, 2026 — Author-confirmed Water Elemental Magnitude MR erosion (LATEST)

**Water status thresholds are no longer numerically open:** A lawful Water-element magnitude condition reaching **1× applicable Water-typed Status Resistance** reduces **applicable Magic Resistance by 15%**. At **4× Water-typed SR**, it reduces **applicable MR by 30% total**, **not** a cumulative 45% reduction. Water specifically erodes MR, **not** AR, and does not impose the ordinary half-SR Fire-style Pressure Point by default. The established status magnitude accumulation/three-turn refresh applies. The detailed typed SR formula, MR percentage rounding, erosion expiry/recovery, and whether reducing MR immediately changes the current Water SR threshold remain **OPEN**; do not invent a circular status escalation. See `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

---

## November 18, 2026 — Smoke sightline simplified to one 5-foot tile (LATEST AUTHOR CORRECTION)

**Supersedes all earlier escalating/tripling smoke accuracy penalties and nine-step sight-loss threshold.** When a relevant attack line passes through **one tile / 5 feet of smoke**, the attacker can still see the target but suffers **one harder Naked-d20 accuracy category**. **More than one smoke tile / 5 feet along that line blocks line of sight**, rather than adding more incremental accuracy categories. Use smoke along the line of sight, not unrelated cloud area. Existing separately approved unseen-target guess-a-tile and 50/50 single-target provisional resolution remain subject to legitimate targeting rules; precise blocked-sight targeting and post-guess procedure remain to be reconciled. Fire-to-smoke conversion, one smoke tile generated per active fire tile per round, Pyromancer smoke breathing and pending Endurance hazards remain unchanged. See `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

---

## November 18, 2026 — Smoke visibility and combat penalty (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

Smoke penalizes eligible attack accuracy by **one Naked-d20 difficulty category** starting at **one qualifying smoke tile**, with a further harder category **each time the qualifying number of smoke tiles triples** (thresholds **1/3/9/27/81/243/729/2187/6561** for **1–9** steps). After nine steps, the target is no longer visible through smoke. Attacker may **guess a target tile**, then if correct a **single-target attack gets a 50% chance to proceed to ordinary attack resolution**. Ordinary category clamping and the meaning of 'normally' after successful guessing need reconciliation; do not secretly invent new d20 categories. Count of smoke relevant to one line of attack, environmental smoke layering and special senses are OPEN. Non-Pyromancers usually require minutes of smoke breathing before respiratory problems (full environmental END rules forthcoming), whereas Pyromancers can breathe pure oxygen-free smoke. See `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

---

## November 18, 2026 — Typhoon penalties, fire ignition and fractional spread (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Typhoon:** Each creature in a Typhoon suffers **one Pressure Point** and **one category harder** ordinary Naked-d20 attack accuracy (author's “−1 accuracy category”), including casters benefiting from rain. Whether the condition's PP stacks with existing PP sources, precisely when it applies/expires and interactions with other weather penalties remain OPEN; no automatic damage or new attack is granted. Each Typhoon rain tile counts as **one actual puddle** for Wakecraft, with **three puddles = one full tile's worth of liquid medium**. See `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

**Fire at round start:** Existing **25%-of-current-burning-fire-tiles** spreading into suitable fuel remains, in wind direction outside and DM-adjudicated/randomly inside. **Accumulate fractional growth** across rounds; do not round a lone fire tile's 0.25 growth into a guaranteed second tile each round. Creatures exposed adjacent to newly spread fire roll a **d4: a result of 1 ignites, results 2–4 avoid ignition**. This is a 25% chance per qualifying exposure, with **basic, generally non-scaling environmental fire harm**. The ignition damage amount, duration and its specific interface with Elemental Magnitude and existing Burning/Smoldering statuses remain undecided; do not infer weapon-enchantment Power for a mundane fire hazard.

**Medium-seizure check:** An eligible defending caster attempting to take over a spell/medium uses **Naked-d20 Average 8+ at equal effective spell Power (65%)**, modified by attacking/defending Power ratio: **2×/4×/12×** relative Power shifts **1/2/3 difficulty categories**, easier if the seizing defender is stronger, harder if the outgoing side is stronger, clamped to ordinary Certain/Easy/Average/Hard/Very Hard/Impossible. Contributors on each side add compatible, lawful spell Power; defensive casting implement bonus matters for defenders, with exact Power formula still OPEN. At 4× or 12× defending advantage, the current six categories already clamp to Certain (same 100% outcome), an acknowledged calibration consequence. Previously deterministic spell seizure Power thresholds are superseded by a chance check, while distinctions and action costs for full spell versus medium seizure need detailed reconciliation.

---

## November 18, 2026 — Environmental fire spread, smoke production and basic ignition (LATEST AUTHOR DIRECTION)

**New environmental growth rule:** At the **beginning of each round**, genuine burning fire can propagate into available flammable material at **25% of its existing fire-tile count**: follow the wind's direction outside; the DM determines a random direction for indoor spread. Without flammable fuel or a legitimate route, no additional fire is conjured. **Each active tile of fire produces one tile of smoke per round**. Existing smoke can accumulate where it is not dispersed, facilitating appropriate learned smoke Pyromancy; exact smoke lifetime, ventilation and phase ordering remain open.

**Edge of fire:** Creatures adjacent to the area where fire is spreading must roll a **d4** against catching fire. This exposure is a **basic, generally non-scaling environmental fire-damage effect**. Neither the d4 result that ignites, the damage amount/cadence, the spread rounding for small fire areas, nor interaction with magical Fire Elemental Magnitude/status thresholds was specified; retain these as OPEN rather than assign a chance or injury stage. Existing magical/weapon fire and their approved typed SR, accumulation and Shimmer Guard effects survive. Fire expands according to real fuel/wind/geometry, not an automatic infinite spread over stone/water.

**Integration:** A caster's spell also needs its own MP and Medium Level; cantrip match flame, portable lantern, and tile-scale fire levels I–VII follow `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`. Medium is not free to another caster while already committed to an active spell without legal medium seizure. No bonus attack or Channel Limit is granted.

## November 18, 2026 — Spellcasting and two-action Channel Limit integration (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**New official casting allocation:** Casting a spell using **one action** is limited to **floor(the caster's applicable full Channel Limit / 2)** MP of throughput. A spell cast using **both actions** can use the **full Channel Limit**. Both operate inside the shared turn-scale Channel Limit already established for prepared defenses and related channeling, and do not restore spent MP. One combatant still has **two actions** and **no more than one offensive attack action per turn**. A magical attack and a weapon attack cannot become separate offensive actions merely because each might individually cost one action. A martial combatant may nevertheless combine a lawful magic/Craft action with a lawful martial action; the new per-action cap governs the casting action, even when no physical action accompanies it. Exact combined action procedures and channel accounting for multiple independent nonoffensive one-action castings remain open. Do not alter existing Full Attack, Quick Attack, Parry, preparation or special Wyrmcraft exceptions without additional approvals. See `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

**Power balance direction:** Physical and magical offensive comparisons are to respect four character-development levers: effective attribute, weapon or Craft-specific casting implement, enchantment, and combat Craft. Implement potency and spell damage formulas are **not yet approved**. Offensive cantrips should be inferior to comparable weapon attacks, while regular mana-costing single-target spells remain effective at high levels. Energy spells deliver eligible Elemental Magnitude/status and **do not gain a universal extra energy damage multiplier**; direct physical impacts generated by spells still meet physical AR as applicable. Wider high-output Pyromancy, focused Psychic offense and intermediate Wakecraft carry different tactical liabilities. No percent-to-weapon damage ladder or spell MP price is canonized.

## November 18, 2026 — Unified elemental Power update APPROVED (LATEST)

All eligible elemental weapon enchantments now deliver approved Power **I–VI = 430 / 1,350 / 3,800 / 5,800 / 8,100 / 15,000** on each genuine lawful elemental contact. This **supersedes** older Fire I–V **226 / 554 / 1,335 / 2,456 / 3,914**, as well as below warnings that the stronger common ladder or Tier VI delivered Power remains unapproved. This changes delivered elemental Power only; approved Fire 0.5× / >1× / ≥4× SR thresholds, injury cadence, three-turn accumulation/refresh, interaction with Shimmer Guard, and genuine delivery still apply. The separate proposed `(END + 0.65MR)^2 × R_element` typed-SR combination is NOT approved; the MR and named elemental-resistance contribution to typed effective SR remains approved in principle with numerical integration open. Refer to `09_Equipment_and_Economy.md` and `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`.

## November 18, 2026 — Typed status resistance and new Ice/Lightning/Hex control (AUTHOR DIRECTIONS; NUMBERS OPEN)

Base **SR = effective END²** remains approved, but applicable **MR and specific Elemental Resistance must increase effective SR against that element**; the combination equation is not yet approved. Elemental weapons all deliver the same Power at the same effect tier; earlier per-element Power discounts are not approved. Ice at 1× SR reduces SPD by 15% and halves movement tiles; 4× Frozen prevents ordinary action but permits magic or reduction of stored Ice by effective STR² per turn; 12× Entombed forbids self-escape. Lightning builds inert Static; discharging **below 1× Lightning SR has no Shocked effect**, at 1× Shocked lasts 3 rounds and subtracts one action per turn (negating an in-progress action if it applies during that action), at 4× also Stunned one round and Trapped for that round. Hex drains personal and Leywell MP, causes damage when personal MP is depleted, and at 4× ends ongoing spell/enchantment effects and drains more MP. Detailed duration/action interactions and numerical drain/damage remain open. Full discussion and candidate calculations in `35_Magical_Media_and_Contests.md`. **Do not use unapproved candidate SR coefficient or Power values as operative canon.**

## November 18, 2026 — Status Resistance, Burning Edge and fire injury cadence (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**Status Resistance (SR) = (effective END)²**, including the full value of eligible equipment END and the usual effective-attribute modifiers. Elemental Magnitude is now **the total eligible elemental Power actually delivered to and accumulated on a target in an element-specific status condition lasting three turns**, not the previous standalone elemental intensity/contact ratio. Each subsequent lawful hit of that element increases the accumulated magnitude. Do not include ordinary physical weapon Power unless it genuinely delivers elemental energy; preserve shields, insulation, material defenses, actual real-element pathways and specific Resistances. No extra offensive attack, separate Speed-based ignition roll, second END-based severity roll, or universal Contact Factor is created.

**Fire thresholds and injury:** Below **0.5 × SR**, Smoldering has no mechanical effect. At **0.5 × SR** or higher, the target takes one ongoing self-Pressure Point in total. Above **1 × SR**, the target is Burning and takes **one injury stage every second round spent Burning**. At **4 × SR** or higher, the target is Engulfed and takes **two injury stages each round**. Apply these stages through the normal injury and Shimmer Guard progression. The threshold crossing determines whether fire can inflict ongoing injuries. Each tick then applies its stated stages directly, without another attack roll or Impact-to-Defense comparison.

**Duration and environment:** An elemental condition lasts **three turns** unless cleansed earlier. Real **mundane or magical interventions** can cleanse or worsen an active condition: e.g., dousing fire with water extinguishes it, oil can make fire worse, and metal armor can strengthen lightning when a genuine conductive path exists. This is not permission to reinstate the old universal Contact Factor. **Fire refresh:** An eligible Burning Edge hit adds its delivered Fire Power to the existing Fire Magnitude and resets its remaining duration to **three turns**, whether the target is **Smoldering**, **Burning**, or **Engulfed**. Extinguishing clears the condition early. Refreshing the duration preserves the rounds already spent Burning. The exact within-turn tick and expiration boundaries, refresh for other elements, environmental adjustments and cleansing action costs remain open.

Find the approved Tier I–V Fire Power ladder in [Equipment and Economy](09_Equipment_and_Economy.md). **Balance constraint:** Burning Edge tiers I–V should remain equally effective relative to same-level/similarly equipped peers even while absolute elemental Power increases with progression. Tier VI Mana Blade remains the intentionally exceptional, short-ammunition peak option. Its 24 MP per five-round activation and 60-MP Leywell remain approved. The weapon's five-round powered *activation* is distinct from the three-turn *target status*. Tier VI Fire Power remains open.

## November 18, 2026 — Contact Factor retirement and historical fire ignition and intensity

**Approved:** **SPD determines chance of IGNITION** after lawful Burning Edge contact and a real heat pathway. Once ignited, **END and specific Fire Resistance determine fire intensity/initial injury**, rather than SPD. Fix the ongoing effect from that resolution and let the real flame persist until extinguished/deprived of fuel. **Do not repeat SPD or END rolls each turn** just to decide whether the fire persists or how severe it is. Low SPD increases susceptibility to catching fire but does not itself impose an already-approved numeric multi-round duration. **Smoldering** applies after ignition where damaging heat is resisted: one meaningful self-PP plus a modest Fire Resistance decrease (precise percent OPEN). Preserve lawful initial contact, distinct physical weapon injury and material protections.

**Removed:** Universal *Contact Factor* is no longer a rule or future standard multiplier; earlier ×1.25 metal fire-contact and ×0.5 insulation tables are draft history only. Ordinary insulation and equipment-specific elemental Resistance already address protection, with no double counting. **Specific exception:** Lightning may gain a defined advantage against a wearer of **actually conductive metal armor** where a credible electrical path reaches the target; exact bonus, grounding and interaction with existing Resistances are NOT approved yet. Keep initial ignition and relative-damage rules compatible with peer-level parity, non-multiplicative physical Weapon Rating and no free added attack.

## November 18, 2026 — Fire ignition versus persistent burn (HISTORICAL)

**Latest author correction supersedes the previous afterburn defense direction:** Burning Edge uses **SPD to determine CHANCE OF IGNITION** after lawful physical contact, **not** to compute how long an already-burning target stays aflame. Resolve actual contact, physically valid heat/fuel pathway and initial relevant Fire Resistance, including whether initial fire damage is resisted. When fire has established itself, it continues until genuinely extinguished/its fuel runs out; **no repeated SPD/END stat calculation every round for fire duration**. An ongoing target fire is not automatically ended when the weapon's own five-round enchantment activation expires. If initial fire damage is resisted but smolder takes hold, **Smoldering** should impose **a modest Fire-specific Resistance reduction** and **one meaningful self-applied PP** until extinguished, not an automatic extra wound or newly granted PP each turn. Numeric Fire Resistance reduction and ignition algorithm remain proposals.

**Peer-relative potency target:** Ordinary enchantment tiers I–V should preserve **roughly equal relative fire damage/severity versus appropriately developed same-tier Hero targets**; do not make successively higher tiers automatically inflict more severe status against their own peers, or multiply the wielder's physical Power. A higher-tier weapon can still outperform lower-level defenses. The old rank-I–V contact coefficients, categorical status caps, and assistant's recently suggested SPD-based 0–3-round burning duration are not operative canon. A generic multiplicative *Contact Factor* was a noncanon transmission approximation; physical pathways, insulation, specific Resistance and struck objects still apply. No change to combat action economy, legal weapon contact, guard, or the specific fire Mana Blade's Tier VI exceptional resource limits.

## November 18, 2026 — Burning Edge continued-fire SPD defense (HISTORICAL)

Burning Edge is the fire-element weapon enchantment family's author-selected name. Retain previously approved **Conditional Contact Effect** and Elemental Magnitude: first resolve the normal lawful attack/contact, with real elemental pathway, insulation, specific protections and no contact rider on a complete dodge. The user specifically approves the design distinction that **the continuing afterburning effect is defended against using Speed/effective SPD, not repeatedly with END**. Initial contact ignition remains an Elemental Magnitude and appropriate initial fire-resistance matter. Do not introduce a free second offensive action or multiply a sword's physical Power. Standard I–V physical blade enchantments differ from the separately approved **Tier VI Mana Blade whose blade is entirely fire while powered**. All numerical Fire Magnitude, exact afterburn timing/effects and elemental injuries remain **NOT APPROVED** until the individual Burning Edge model is selected.

## November 18, 2026 — Small-item swaps and armor donning times (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**One-action ring exchange:** Remove one worn ring and equip its replacement, or similarly change an ordinary accessible small accessory, as **one combat action total**. This directly resolves the prior ambiguity about separate remove-plus-equip actions. The user still has only **two active enchanted-ring equipment slots** and obeys ordinary attack/action limits. Item access, actual hands and special constraints still matter. Carrying dozens of artillery rings does not make them simultaneously equipped.

**Armor donning:** Light armor takes **2 combat actions** to equip; Medium armor takes **2 minutes**; Heavy armor takes **10 minutes**. No full suit's protections are granted merely by starting to put it on. Exact **doffing**, helpers and interruptions are **open**, not silently assigned the same times. Special wearable magical transformation rules already approved do not become full undressing/donning sequences.

**Tier VI Mana Blade:** Whole fire-made sword blade is Tier VI, vanishes when unpowered, and requires real medium plus Leywell mana. Previous rank-V whole elemental weapon wording is superseded in tier assignment; its actual physical-versus-elemental hit resolution remains unapproved. An energized Mana Blade is not a free additional attack. Tier VI Leywell capacity is **60 MP**, and Powerful activation cost is **24 MP per standard five-round activation** under the newest author-agreed schedule.

## November 18, 2026 — One-action item handling and ready thrown weapons (LATEST AUTHOR APPROVAL)

**One ordinary interaction action:** Taking/retrieving an accessible carried or nearby item, or performing an ordinary physical environmental interaction such as opening a door, costs **one action** unless a more specific lawful procedure applies. One action can **draw or sheathe up to two eligible physically accessible weapons**. This is not an automatic bypass of reachability, locks, constraints, weapon readiness, body anatomy, or an exceptional item procedure.

**Thrown weapons:** When the combatant has a free hand or can free one (e.g. temporarily take one hand off a held two-handed weapon), **one ready throwable weapon stored in a bandolier/quiver can be drawn and thrown as ONE action**. This is one legal offensive attack action, not a bonus attack or a new default ranged-weapon reload rule. Maintain the approved **maximum one offensive attack action per turn**, normal legal throw/attack form and any specifically applicable one-action attack conditions. Ordinary weapon handling, positioning and number of actions remain binding.

**Equipped-item changes:** Equipping or removing rings and other gear is meaningful physical interaction, not free activation. Cycling a collection of magical artillery rings requires spent interaction actions and still only **two active equipped-ring slots**. The exact remove-and-replace sequence for rings is underdefined, so do not silently assume an extra free substitution, a numeric fast-swap feat, or an independently approved one-action entire ring exchange. Separate free **passive enchantment activation** does not waive the physical action needed to change the worn item. Active magical attacks remain bound by one offensive action and ordinary 1–2 action costs; prepared enchanted defense remains bound by its already approved secretly committed action/trigger model.

## November 18, 2026 — Secretly prepared active enchanted defense (AUTHOR APPROVED)

**Active defensive enchantments follow the existing prepared defensive-action model (Parry and applicable prepared magical defenses), not an on-demand free reaction.** On the defender's own turn, **secretly commit the required one or two combat actions**, identify the specific equipped defensive enchantment, and declare a **narrow, legal, outcome-independent trigger** in advance. The prepared enchantment takes effect at its lawful response opportunity if that trigger occurs and all requirements (including appropriate Leywell power, physical medium where relevant, and ordinary positioning/eligibility) hold. The defender cannot wait to see whether the attack would hit or deal damage before choosing the trigger or defense.

**Examples:** “The next attack against this named ally” or “the next attack made by this specified enemy” may qualify when the item actually supports that coverage. “The next attack that hits me” is not eligible as an outcome-dependent trigger. Follow the already approved preparation/expiry and relevant action limitations of the defensive framework being used; no additional defensive action, free attack, or reserved action beyond the existing two-action economy is created. Magical defense mechanics are not automatically granted the Parry-specific +1/+2 hit category, Pressure Point rewards, cooldown, or shield routing unless the individual enchantment explicitly earns such behavior under its actual approved rules.

**Distinction preserved:** Action-free passive modifications of an existing defense (e.g., an eligible preactivated reinforcement effect) remain passive; an enchantment that **independently performs an active defensive maneuver** consumes the preparatory action(s) and obeys the secret-condition system. The five-round duration of ordinary magical enhancements is distinct from the time window of a prepared, triggered active defense. Further item-specific duration on activation, expenditure timing if a trigger never occurs, trigger expiry, response scheduling, multiple prepared effects and coordination with the single prepared spell constraint require explicit calibration. They are **not** authorization for unlimited simultaneous active defensive reactions.

## November 16, 2026 — Reach distances and Technique-based movement control APPROVED (LATEST)

**Author-approved reach and distance:** Each combat grid tile is **5 feet** for these melee reach distances. **Short Reach** can attack at **1 tile / 5 feet** only. **Medium Reach** can attack at **1 tile normally**, or **2 tiles / 10 feet** with **one harder Naked-d20 hit-difficulty category** for lunging (clamp using the existing six categories); there is no blanket extra damage, action or movement entitlement. **Long Reach** can attack at **1 or 2 tiles normally**, subject to ordinary weapon handling, obstacles and lawful attack forms. These are maximum ordinary distance bands; physically impossible swings, line geometry and authentic grapples remain restricted by their independent rules. Being adjacent to a Long weapon is **not** itself a grapple or a ban on its ordinary attack.

**Author-approved spatial control zones:** A combatant using a legal, ready **Short- or Medium-Reach weapon can contest movement only at the 1-tile distance**. A combatant using a legal, ready **Long-Reach weapon can contest movement only at the 2-tile distance**, **never merely because an enemy is adjacent at 1 tile**. This is a separate zone of movement control, not an alteration of where the weapon may make its ordinary attacks; thus a Medium weapon may attack by lunging at 2 tiles but does not control movement there. This also prevents a Long-reach fighter from continually locking down a foe who has already closed adjacent.

**Movement and entry procedure:** A meaningful defender's control zone can require a **contested Technique d20 check** when a moving combatant **crosses out of that zone**, rather than letting them run freely past an established frontline. **Entering inside a defended Long weapon's 2-tile reach** (crossing from 2 tiles to adjacent 1 tile) likewise requires a Technique entry check when the defender can genuinely maintain that 2-tile barrier; it is **not** resolved by the former effective-SPD ratio gates. A reach-controlling defender must have an actual available weapon, credible geometry and an actionable threat; a stunned/otherwise incapable, obstructed or meaningfully pressured defender is not entitled to a free control check by mere proximity. Natural movement **within** a Short/Medium 1-tile engagement does not generate repeated checks merely for repositioning without crossing an actual controlled boundary. For ordinary withdrawal or passage, **successful Technique** permits movement within the mover's otherwise legal available movement tiles; **failure stops the attempted crossing at the contested boundary**, without granting a free damaging strike or grapple. Resolve with a **single relevant movement contest against the strongest credible controller**, rather than a separate roll for every nearby combatant; real additional defenders can influence the contest through existing Pressure principles, without inventing a numerical bonus or automatic PP per body.

**No automatic grapple or opportunity attack:** Crossing into adjacency, slipping past an opponent, or succeeding on movement Technique **never automatically initiates a grapple** and **never grants a no-action grapple attempt**. A grapple is still its separately approved committed offensive maneuver and **one Technique check**, subject to actual reach and opportunity. No default Pathfinder-style attack of opportunity, free wound, or additional offensive action is created.

**Supersession and open implementation:** The former **effective-SPD defender/approacher 1.25×/1.5× Long-versus-Short reach-entry gates are superseded** by Technique-based entry. Existing Combat Speed/accuracy/evasion, initiative, Dash movement and ordinary ability-to-move rules do not change. **Still unresolved:** the exact opposed-Technique score-to-Naked-d20 category conversion, precise effect of Pressure and multiple controllers, cost/timing of check relative to ordinary movement expenditure, edge cases for involuntary displacement, and how repeated crossings in one turn are limited. These are implementation/calibration questions, not permission to revert to SPD gates or create free attacks. No additive Weapon Rating or other numerical damage model is approved by this reach update.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Dash movement-tile multipliers and upward rounding APPROVED (LATEST)

**Latest author-approved Dash movement rule (two-action tabletop pilot):** Determine the combatant's ordinary movement-tile allowance, **M**, before Dash bonuses. A **Dash / Half Dash (1 action)** permits movement up to **ceil(M × 1.5) tiles** (+50%); a **Double Dash / Full Dash (2 actions)** permits movement up to **ceil(M × 2) tiles** (+100%). **Always round fractional movement tiles UP after applying the multiplier**, not down or at an intermediate step. Thus, 5 ordinary tiles become 8 on Dash or 10 on Double Dash; 7 ordinary tiles become 11 or 14.

**Do not derive additional movement tiles by recalculating SPD-to-tile thresholds from Dash's temporary increased Combat Speed.** The existing distinct +25% Combat Speed for Half Dash and +50% for Full Dash still last until the start of the user's next turn, under their existing combat accuracy/evasion rules; those temporary bonuses are not another movement-tile multiplier. Dash still does not grant extra attacks, improve initiative, ignore obstacles or reach control, or bypass other legal positioning restrictions. This **supersedes** the November 15 pilot language stating that Dash does not multiply actual movement distance. **Base movement-tile allowance and SPD thresholds remain uncalibrated/unapproved**; this update does not approve a 5-tile base, new SPD brackets, or a battlefield-relative speed formula.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple initiation uses ONE Technique check (ACTION PROCEDURE ACTIVE; HISTORICAL STAT SUPERSEDED NOV22)

**Latest explicit author approval:** Replace the earlier **ordinary attack accuracy check for grapple initiation** with **one Technique d20 check** to establish the grapple, using the then-approved, **now-superseded** universal **Technique = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX** statistic (use **max(effective STR, effective DEX)** under the November 22 rule). No separate attack-roll-plus-Technique double check is required. Before the Technique check, the initiating combatant must have a **genuine physical opportunity to reach and establish a hold**, respecting approved reach control, intervening geometry, movement, relative SPD and other legal opposition. Merely occupying an adjacent square or declaring a grapple is not automatic entry. Grapple initiation remains a **committed offensive maneuver counting against the turn's one offensive-action limit**, never a free additional attack, bonus strike or automatic injury. A successful eligible Technique check establishes the grapple and existing grapple-range consequences.

**Not yet approved:** Exact ratio of opposing Technique scores to six Naked-d20 check categories for initiation/escape, cost in the two-action economy (one or two actions), amount/duration/stacking of relevant Pressure, retry limits and other maneuvers (trips, disarms, shoves, throws). The previous attack-check initiation requirement is explicitly **superseded**. Existing approved escape-on-turn, failed escape cannot leave square / +1 incoming PP, and Pressure making escape harder remain unchanged. No proposed additive Weapon Rating or movement-tile rule is approved by this update.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Technique statistic and Pressure-sensitive grapple escape (HISTORICAL FORMULA; SUPERSEDED NOV22)

**Author's explicit design decision:** Generalize the approved grapple-control score into the combat statistic **Technique = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX** (Strength counts twice), using the existing effective race-adjusted attributes. Grapple control is an application of Technique, rather than an independent differently scaled number. Non-damaging combat-control maneuvers such as **trips, disarms, grapples, shoves and throws** are intended members of the Technique family. Their individual procedures and exact secondary effects **are not yet approved**; the author explicitly deferred designing them. Neither Technique nor its maneuver family alters ordinary attack Power, approved weapon F, or ordinary SPD+DEX/4 evasion.

**Approved escape principle:** **Meaningful net Pressure on a grapple escaper makes escaping harder**, not easier. The ordinary existing Pressure tiers (net 0 Open, net 1–2 Pressured, net 3+ Extreme) provide the intended integration; the proposed **numerical implementation** is 0 / +1 / +2 harder d20 difficulty categories respectively on an otherwise eligible escape check, with standard chart clamping. This exact application to escape has been recommended but **not separately numerically approved**. Only genuine distinct Pressure sources count; the mere existence of the grapple should not be counted as an additional automatically granted PP without authorization. The already approved +1 incoming PP for a failed escape remains.

**Pending user selection:** The assistant recommends initiating a grapple by **one Technique d20 check after lawful reach-entry/physical opportunity** instead of an ordinary accuracy attack check, while consuming the existing limit of one offensive action. This is **not yet approved**; the previously authorized requirement for a successful grapple-initiation attack check remains in force until user decides. Exact Technique-relative d20 difficulty mapping, whether/how the established escape mapping applies to initiation, timing, action costs, failed-escape Pressure duration, repeated attempts and third-party intervention remain open. Do not require two sequential attack-plus-Technique rolls unless separately approved.

---

## November 16, 2026 — Grapple Control STR-weighted formula (HISTORICAL; SUPERSEDED NOV22)

**Latest explicit author approval:** For grapple control and opposed escape calibration use **`G = 2 × effective STR + effective DEX`** for each involved combatant. Strength contributes twice as much as Dexterity; this replaces the earlier equal-weight `STR + DEX` candidate. Preserve race-adjusted effective attributes, fractional values, and the approved ordinary combat accuracy/evasion calculation separately. This approval authorizes the **attribute-weighting formula**, not the previously proposed exact `G_holder/G_escaper` six-category mapping, escape action expenditure, pressure duration/stacking, initiation action cost, or other grapple corner cases. Those are still experimental and must be tested before approval. Preserve the November 16 approved grapple-range weapon, shield and failure-Pressure principles immediately below.
